# CNX notes

Notes from the CNX session (reading OpenStax's CNXML directly, `unpack-cnx.py`), for Rob to pass on to the other sessions. Kept beside the code it's about, on `main` once the work is. Last revised 2026-10-27, for `main`: LaTeX's picture mode drawn by pict2e in every PDF, and `picture_paths: off` (item 87); a light rule under each row of the tables OpenStax's math books rule so (item 88); `math.display_style_in_text` (item 89); a step's display style written down (item 90); code at the text's size, a block too wide fitted to its line (item 91); and what the reviews of them found (item 92).

The `cnx` branch's history is the WIP commits, kept in the session's clone. Everything CNX-specific is in `bin/unpack-cnx.py`, `lib/cnx/` (a package: `repo`, `cnxml`, `mathml`, `assemble`, `exercises`, `bake/` with `core`, `directions`, `injected`, `recipes`, `strings`, then `pages`, `emit`, `steptables`, `mathtex`, `project`, `unpack`, and `compare` for the utilities), `util/cnx-compare-word.py`, `util/cnx-compare-cookbook.py`, `util/cnx-compare-assembly.py`, `tests/run-cnx-tests.py`, and `docs/cnx-input.md`. Outside them: one line in `tests/run-portability-test.py` (it globbed `lib/*.py` only, so a package under `lib/` went unchecked), a row in `README.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/formats.md`, and `docs/testing.md`, three in `docs/utilities.md`, and the suite in `tests/run-all.sh`.
- **Every style OpenStax bakes a book in is baked**: fifty-six, all of cookbook's recipes but `dummy` (its test), `super` (a skeleton for a new book), and `web-generic`, with `post_bake`, which runs after every recipe; two of the styles are another's recipe, as cookbook's bake script maps them (`ap-physics-2e` is ap-physics, `otto-book` chemistry). The social sciences and business: economics, statistics (the two college books and the high school *Statistics*), marketing, sociology, information-systems, data-science, nursing-external (*Pharmacology for Nurses*, *Nutrition for Nurses*, *Population Health for Nurses*), python (*Introduction to Python Programming*), accounting (both volumes of *Principles of Accounting*), bca (*Workplace Software and Skills*), business-ethics (*Business Ethics*, *Business Law I Essentials*, *Introduction to Intellectual Property*), entrepreneurship, finance (*Principles of Finance*), intro-business (*Introduction to Business*), and principles-management (*Principles of Management*, *Organizational Behavior*). Math: precalculus (*College Algebra 2e*, *Algebra and Trigonometry 2e*, *Precalculus 2e*), precalculus-coreq (*College Algebra with Corequisite Support 2e*), calculus (*Calculus*, Volumes 1–3), dev-math (*Prealgebra 2e*, *Elementary Algebra 2e*, *Intermediate Algebra 2e*), contemporary-math (*Contemporary Mathematics*), algebra-1 (*Algebra 1*, the high school book), and additive-manufacturing (*Additive Manufacturing Essentials*). The humanities: psychology (*Psychology 2e*), lifespan-development (*Lifespan Development*), anthropology (*Introduction to Anthropology*), political-science (*Introduction to Political Science*), american-government (*American Government 4e*), history (*U.S. History*), world-history (*World History*, Volumes 1 and 2), philosophy (*Introduction to Philosophy 2e*), english-composition (*Writing Guide with Handbook*), college-success (*College Success 2e*, *College Success Concise*), hs-college-success (*Preparing for College Success*), and computer-science (*Introduction to Computer Science*, whose repository isn't public, so it's checked on cookbook's sample alone). The sciences: biology (*Biology 2e*, *Concepts of Biology*), anatomy (*Anatomy and Physiology 2e*), microbiology (*Microbiology*), chemistry (*Chemistry 2e*, *Chemistry: Atoms First 2e*), astronomy (*Astronomy 2e*), college-physics-2e (*College Physics 2e*; college-physics, its first edition's, is in no repository now and checked on its sample), and u-physics (*University Physics*, Volumes 1–3). New in this round: the AP editions, ap-biology (*Biology for AP® Courses*), ap-history (*Life, Liberty, and the Pursuit of Happiness*), and ap-physics (*College Physics for AP® Courses 2e*); hs-physics (*Physics*, the high school book); neuroscience (*Introduction to Behavioral Neuroscience*); organic-chemistry (*Organic Chemistry*); nursing-internal (*Clinical Nursing Skills*, whose repository isn't public); and the five Polish editions, pl-economics (*Mikroekonomia – Podstawy*, *Makroekonomia - Podstawy*), pl-psychology (*Psychologia*), pl-u-physics (*Fizyka dla szkół wyższych*, Volumes 1–3), and pl-marketing and pl-nursing, whose repositories aren't public. Seventy-six books in all, in 49 repositories, with the five of the economics bundle, every repository public: the business and math ones as listed before (`osbooks-nursing-external-bundle`, `-introduction-python-programming`, `-principles-accounting-bundle`, `-principles-finance`, `-business-ethics`, `-business-law`, `-introduction-intellectual-property`, `-entrepreneurship`, `-introduction-business`, `-principles-of-management`, `-workplace-software-skills`, `-college-algebra-bundle`, `-calculus-bundle`, `-prealgebra-bundle`, `-contemporary-mathematics`, `-algebra-1`, `-statistics`, `-additive-manufacturing`), the humanities and science ones (`osbooks-psychology`, `-lifespan-development`, `-introduction-anthropology`, `-introduction-political-science`, `-american-government`, `-us-history`, `-world-history`, `-introduction-philosophy`, `-writing-guide`, `-college-success-bundle`, `-biology-bundle`, `-anatomy-physiology`, `-microbiology`, `-chemistry-bundle`, `-astronomy`, `-college-physics-bundle`, `-university-physics-bundle`), and now `osbooks-life-liberty-pursuit-happiness`, `-physics`, `-neuroscience`, `-organic-chemistry`, `-psychologia`, `-mikroekonomia`, `-makroekonomia`, and `-fizyka-bundle`. Each unpacks in seconds (*Anatomy and Physiology 2e*'s 336 pages in 7, *Algebra 1*'s 977 in 14). A Connexions archive book gets a plain bake (numbers within each page, nothing moved), as would a book in a style cookbook adds later.
- **Not done**: nothing of cookbook's but `super` and `web-generic`, which bake no book. Content MathML is made presentation MathML in its simple forms (names, numbers, relations, arithmetic, powers, roots, functions applied), and anything else reported as `content-mathml`: one formula in the seventeen repositories first checked is content MathML outside an annotation, Introductory Statistics' "<" written `<reln><lt/></reln>`, and it converts.

## Fixes for `main`

The first five are 33333f1 to 7da9fba, with the slides session's follow-up 59dffcf. The other six went out as `main-fixes.mbox` and `main-fixes-2.mbox`; the slides session reviewed them and rewrote them on 2ac2bff as `cnx-fixes.mbox` (7c751d3 to cd59247), with four commits of its own after them, which Rob pushed (ac683cc), listed after the six.

1. **Tables: a split table's first part keeps the table's id.** `tables.bands: split` numbered every part's id (`costs-1`), so `#costs` named nothing: 12 links to 6 tables in Economics 3e.
2. **Captions: a table's own caption that is its label alone is described.** `<caption>Table 3.8</caption>` or Markdown's `Table: Table 3.8` was neither described from `table-captions.csv` nor reported; 62 tables in Economics 3e.
3. **Titles: no word of a missing number when the title has it.** The "keep their declared title without the number" message fired when the title was the numbered heading itself (142 pages).
4. **Frames: a size with a fraction of a pixel is rounded.** `<iframe height="371.4">`, as OpenStax sizes every frame, became `style="height: 371.4"`, which vnu rejects and browsers ignore. The unpacker rounds a frame's size the same way, a half up.
5. **Docs: the EPUB input page's `project.yaml` bullet said one thing twice**, half a link between ("…table of contents.md)); there's no group around the whole book…"), left when bb1db19 took out `convert: true`.
6. **PDF: an unnumbered table's caption, and circled letters** (7c751d3; this was `main-pdf-openstax.mbox`). `\ext@none` is defined and such a caption set without a label; New Computer Modern Math is the fallbacks' last when it's installed (PANDOC-NOTES, and the PDF item under open questions).
7. **PDF: a tagged build needs LaTeX 2026-06-01** (d790ecc). `MINIMUM_LATEX` is a constant in `lib/latexbuild.py`, not a setting, and was 2025-11-01. With 2025-11-01, 14 of the suites' checks fail; with 2026-06-01, none; and no LaTeX release falls between (TeX Live versions, below). It isn't a CNX matter: the failures are in the PDF target, which a CNX book's PDF goes through like any other, and in the LaTeX-source path. The message says `tlmgr update --self --all` brings an earlier TeX Live 2026 up to date.
8. **Source copies: a key its LaTeX doesn't know is skipped** (27742f2). The `source` target's copy of a LaTeX book, which its author builds with their own LaTeX, stopped pdfLaTeX on a tikzpicture's `alt` or `artifact` with the pgf here ("I do not know the key '/tikz/alt'"; only tagging's tikz support defined them; pgf 3.1.12, 2026-08-01, newer than Debian's snapshot of TeX Live 2026 here, defines them itself, as the slides session found), on a picture's before 2026-06-01, whose `\picture` took no options, and on an image's `artifact` with TeX Live 2023's graphicx. A block before each master's `\begin{document}` gives such a key one that does nothing and leaves a known one alone: checked with TeX Live 2023, 2025, and 2026, untagged and tagged, and on GIAM, whose copy builds to its 434 pages with each, where 2025's stopped at its pictures without the block. The source suite's book has a tikzpicture now; without the block, its copy fails to build on 2026.
9. **HTML sources: another site's link fragment is left as it is** (c19fe0e). `html-source.lua`'s `fix_fragment`, which makes a link's fragment match the id it names on our own pages (an id with spaces made one with hyphens), did the same to every link's: a text fragment on another site (`#:~:text=` and words with their escapes, `%20` for a space, `%25` for a percent sign) was decoded and its spaces made hyphens, `…is-excellent-(44%-vs`, a lone `%` the Nu checker and epubcheck reject, and words a browser can't find. Found converting *Lifespan Development* (4 links) and *Introduction to Political Science* (1); any HTML source with such a link had it. A link with a scheme is left as it is now.
10. **Math from text: a command before a Greek letter is set apart from it** (f408aab). With `math.from_text`, *Chemistry 2e*'s *q* = *m* × *c* × Δ*T*, whose × the source writes as an equation, became `q=m\times c\timesΔT`, which texmath reads as one command it doesn't know (PANDOC-NOTES), so the page and the EPUB showed the TeX (`formula-shown-as-tex`, 2 formulas). `join_tex` in `math-repair.lua` spaced a command from a letter after it, but Lua's `%a` is ASCII; a character that isn't ASCII gets the space too now. Not a CNX matter: any source with a × typed as an equation beside a Greek letter typed as text had it.
11. **Tables: a split leaves no band without rows** (cd59247). `tables.bands: split` (the default for HTML, EPUB, and PDF) dropped a band with no row under it before the next band or the table's end, with the part it would have headed, and nothing said so. The guess took two shapes for such bands. A note one cell across a table's foot: *Principles of Economics 3e*'s Table 11.1 lost its conclusion ("Then the four-firm concentration ratio is 16 + 10 + 8 + 6 = 40."), and five more tables in the Word corpus lost their last rows (four in *Clinical Nursing Skills*, one in *Principles of Data Science*), measured with the pre-pass on the seven books of the Word corpus; a table with no other band was captioned "Part 1" as well. And a row repeating row 1 but for its first cell, the sociology book's groups: every row of the *Writing Guide*'s Table 15.2, a graphic organizer, is one, so the whole table went, found by its link going nowhere (`link-to-missing-fragment`). A band with nothing under it stays a row now, guessed or declared, and the guess names neither shape; the 27 other guessed splits in the corpus are as they were. Released code: 0.9's outputs of such tables are missing those rows.

The slides session's four after them:

- **Tables: a label above a table takes nothing after it** (3efcf14). A table taking a label paragraph above it for its caption also dropped the block after the table, whatever it was, in every release.
- **Tables: a split table keeps its foot** (89d5431), and the guess names no row of an HTML table's foot in `split-at`.
- **Tables: a head one cell across is a band or a title, not a header row** (10e14aa). Pandoc's Word reader puts row 1 in the head when Word's table look formats it, and the split copied the head into every part, so a band there headed them all; and an HTML `<thead>` holding only a title declared a header row, so the first row of data became one once the title was the caption.
- **The CNX fixes followed up** (ac683cc): pgf 3.1.12's TikZ keys, with the LaTeX suite building the keys block against the older definitions; a `//host` link left as written; a fragment's spaces made hyphens and its escaped letters decoded, its other escapes kept (a cross-page `#na%C3%AFve` had named no id where pages are split), the output check finding an id as a browser does; a merged first row over a band the table's title, unless a band-over-band pair is further down; a band over a band composed into the part's caption; warnings only for split rows a person declared.

What these change on the Word corpus is measured below (TextbookImprover observations).

Six more went out as `main-fixes-6.mbox` on 3dfae32, and Rob pushed them unchanged (2889589 to 8e1266b, 2026-10-14): the first two from this round's books, the other four from the rest of the books (TextbookImprover observations), the last at Rob's request. Each check they add fails on 3dfae32 without them:

12. **Tables: a split table's parts repeat no ids.** Each part after the first repeated the header row with the ids it and its cells had in the source, and the table's caption with its ids, and a part headed by a group's own header row (the first row's but for its corner, the group's name) took the corner's words into its caption, ids and all, so a page held an id more than once, invalid HTML and an EPUB file epubcheck rejects (RSC-005): the Polish economics books, 38 of the Nu checker's errors and 58 of epubcheck's. The first part keeps the ids, which a link names, and each part's own header row its own; what's repeated has none, and an anchor that held only an id goes. Any HTML source whose header cells or captions have ids had it.
13. **Markdown target: a table grouped by its own header rows keeps its groups.** Kept whole for a source target (`tables.bands: group`), a table whose groups each open with a header row like the first but for its first cell has a body per group headed by its row, and Pandoc's Markdown writer made those rows ordinary ones of a single body (PANDOC-NOTES): *Introduction to Sociology 3e*'s Table 7.2, unpacked, was a pipe table with an empty header row, which read back with no headers and no groups. It's written as HTML now, as a table with merged cells already was and as the AsciiDoc target writes such a table. Found writing the test for 12. Read back from either, the table was one grouped table, not split, until 2035ada (below).
14. **Tables: a split table's part spans no column it begins no cell in.** A part laid out on fewer columns than the table, each of its cells across two, kept the table's columns, some of which began no cell, invalid HTML (the Nu checker's "Table column 2 established by element th has no cells beginning in it"): *Foundations of Information Systems*' Table 3.7, three tables in normal form, Orders on its four columns and Customers and Addresses on two, 4 errors. Such columns are closed up in each part, a cell across one spanning one fewer and the column's width going to the one before it. The unpacker closes up a whole table's (Found by converting, the round before); this is a part's, which only the split makes.
15. **Links: a scheme in capitals is a scheme.** A link at `Http://…`, as *Introduction to Intellectual Property*'s source writes an address, was a missing file to the output check, and the packager stopped the run ("referenced file(s) are not on disk"); a picture at one stopped it at the media check. Seven places in four files (the packager, the output check, `convert.py`'s media and hand-page checks, and the audit's bare-link check, which didn't report `HTTPS://…` as a bare link) compared a scheme in lowercase only; they compare it lowercased now.
16. **Reports: a quotation is cut between characters, not inside one.** A table's excerpt in `table-captions-missing.csv`, and what `fidelity.csv` quotes of a footnote or an example list, were cut at a count of bytes, which can end inside a character of several (a minus sign is three), and `convert.py` stopped with a UnicodeDecodeError reading the row back: *Elementary Algebra 2e*.
17. **Equation images: re-authoring advice for any source.** A picture of an equation whose alt text is MathSpeak drew "consider re-authoring this as a real equation in the DOCX" whatever the source: *Introduction to Business 2e*, *Principles of Management*, and *Principles of Financial Accounting*, unpacked, have such pictures. It says "in the source" now, naming a Word equation, TeX math, and MathML, as `docs/troubleshooting.md` does.

The slides session's two after them, which settle two questions this file had:

- **Tables: a table grouped by its groups' own header rows splits again** (2035ada). A table of two or more bodies with no head of its own, each body's head a row like the others but for the group's name in its first cell, is banded at those rows, as proposed here, so the Markdown or AsciiDoc copy of *Introduction to Sociology 3e*'s Table 7.2 reads back as the book's own HTML has it, a table per group. A group's own header row heads its part whatever the table's declaration says, where an HTML page's `<th>` cells had declared a header column alone and moved each part's header row into its body.
- **An HTML page's heading gives way to an id the page gave, and renames are reported** (77e8c20). The table-id case (TextbookImprover observations): `htmlrepair.py` marks a heading the page gives no id (`data-ti-auto-id`), `make_ids_unique` renames such a heading wherever it stands, and every other rename is a row of `ids-renamed.csv` (`reports.ids_renamed`), the terminal's warning counting only those. A Markdown heading keeps its id, since `[Costs]` links to it.

The slides session's `notes-groups` (1192f59), which settles a third (TextbookImprover observations): `notes.groups` gives the HTML pages and the EPUB one rule, a top-level entry of the contents, a chapter with all its pages or a page on its own, and in a group of `type: part`, each entry of the part. It's not the rule proposed here, and rightly: the split's groups for a page the split cut and the innermost contents group for any other would have carried the HTML's mistakes for a split book into the EPUB, and broken a chapter cut at its H3s into runs, since the contents nest a section with subsections as a group of its own; and the claim here that the EPUB changed only for parts didn't hold for a single-file book. The unpacker now writes `type: part` on a unit and `notes.numbering: group` (Design decisions).

And one more, `caption-labels.mbox` on b519b71, at Rob's request after the PDF item under open questions, which Rob pushed (da4f1f0, 2026-10-15):

18. **PDF: a caption that carries its own label isn't numbered again.** LaTeX gives every caption a label of its own, so a book whose captions number themselves had two numbers, "Figure 1: Figure 1.1 The real number line" (*College Algebra*'s first chapter, "Figure 2: Figure 1 The real number line"), and a screen reader read both, LaTeX's tagged as the caption's label; and a figure the book marks unnumbered, as OpenStax marks a chapter's opening picture (class `unnumbered`), was numbered, "Figure 1: Credit: Andreas Kambanls". `pdf-target.lua` sets a figure or table whose caption opens with a word `captions.figure_prefixes` or `captions.table_prefixes` names, then a number, and a figure of class `unnumbered` (on the figure, or on its image, where the Markdown reader puts it), in a group with `\OERNoCaptionLabel` (`latexbuild.py`). Tagged, LaTeX's figure caption is its own (`latex-lab-testphase-float.sty`): the `caption/label` socket gets its `noop` plug, and the two tagging sockets around it a plug that only switches paragraph tagging off, starting the paragraph as the label would have, and one that switches it on, so the Caption holds its words and no Lbl. A long table's caption, and an untagged figure's in a book with tables, is the caption package's, told to give none; an untagged figure's in a book without tables, set as the standard classes set one, without its label. The PDF suite gets such captions (a figure's, a long one's, a long table's, an unnumbered figure's), and an untagged book with no table, one of its captions labeled "Exhibit" through `captions.figure_prefixes`; each of its checks fails without its part of the fix, in eleven ways of breaking it, two of them (the tagging sockets left at their defaults, or made `noop`) stopping LaTeX on the long caption. On OpenStax's books, unpacked with this branch and built as PDFs a chapter at a time (TeX Live 2026, no contents): *College Algebra 2e*'s first chapter (12 pages, a PDF of 118) and *Principles of Management*'s (10 pages, 18 in the PDF, its six captions "Exhibit", through `captions.figure_prefixes`) have every caption once, with its own label and no label of LaTeX's, the opening picture's with none, and each figure's Caption holds its words and no Lbl. A Polish book's PDF wasn't built here: this TeX Live has no babel Polish (nor Latin, which *Makroekonomia - Podstawy* marks "per capita" as), and the run stops, saying how to find the package that has it.

And one found building a page of the dev-math books' step tables as a PDF (the step tables, under open questions), `cell-math.mbox` on da4f1f0, which Rob pushed (a494d8b, 2026-10-15):

19. **PDF: a formula with an alignment in a table's cell or caption.** LaTeX's tagging reads a formula whole before setting it, and TeX took a matrix's or an array's `&` for the end of the table's cell ("Forbidden control sequence found while scanning use of `\__math_grab_inline_delim:w`"), or, in a caption, for the table's while nameref read its title. OpenStax's dev-math books check a solution that way in their worked examples' steps, an array of its equations inline in a cell: a multi-column array is in a table's cell on 2 of *Prealgebra 2e*'s pages, 33 of *Elementary Algebra 2e*'s, and 29 of *Intermediate Algebra 2e*'s, and *Elementary Algebra 2e*'s 5.2 stopped LaTeX. So did a page of three cases built from HTML, the array inline in a cell, displayed in a cell, and in a caption. `pdf-target.lua` sets such a formula in braces; with it, each builds, its formula a Formula with its MathML file in its TD or its Caption, and so does 5.2. The PDF suite's book gets such a formula in a cell and in a caption, and its new check fails without the fix, the build stopping. With it on da4f1f0, every suite passes, with both Pythons.

And five from the review of the step tables (`claude/layout-tables-review.md` in the project, 2026-10-15) and the slides session's run of the first on the Word corpus (`claude/layout-tables-word-corpus.md`, 2026-10-15), `layout-tables.mbox` on 7a4e90e, which Rob pushed (64a1200, 2026-10-17). The first was the mailbox's one commit before (made on d21f490, its CHANGELOG, `docs/markdown.md`, and `docs/testing.md` merged with the deck work's), with that run's `layout-head.patch` folded in now; the other four fix what the run, and building the dev-math books' chapters as PDFs, found besides:

20. **A layout table, which every target writes as one.** A table that only lines things up is a layout table when its page says so with `role="presentation"`, or `role="none"`, which `html-source.lua` makes `presentation` as it reads the page; when a LaTeX book tags it as a division of paragraphs (`table/tagging=div`), which `latexsource.py` reads as the declaration, a box tagged so staying a box; and when `table-headers.csv` says `layout`, a new value, which is how a Word table becomes one. `figures-and-tables.lua` writes it with `role="presentation"`, asks no caption of it, looks for no header row, and wraps it in no focusable scroll region, and `page.css` stacks its cells on a narrow screen (WCAG 1.4.10 lets a data table scroll, and a layout table isn't among its exceptions), any caption a line of its own after them, as a wide screen sets it: left a table's caption, it was a column one word wide ("Table" over "3.2" in Chromium at 360 pixels, seen building the before-and-after page of 2026-10-16). It keeps its place in its page's count of tables, and a description the captions sidecar has for it is reported unused. `table-headers-new.csv` lists a page's own as `layout`, the report naming the page as the supplier, the drafted-by column `TI`, as it is for a page's `<th>`, except what the census counts no table, a single cell or pictures alone; a row naming its headers, `none` among them, makes it a data table. The output check and the Word target take `none` as `presentation`. A head it comes with is rows like the others, with no header cell (`layout-head.patch`): Pandoc's Word reader puts a table's first row in its head when Word repeats it as a header or its table look formats it, as it does 881 of the Word corpus's 1,045 data tables, and latex-lab's longtable code makes a head's cells header cells even under the division setting, each a TH outside any TR, which veraPDF's PDF/UA-2 profile rejects (Sect-TH, 63 cells in the run's 29 tables declared `layout`), and the Word target would have marked the row to repeat as a header. The cost is the bold a browser gives a page's own `<th>` in such a table, whose role had already taken the header's meaning away; a `source` copy of the page keeps the author's head, since it edits the role alone, and a row of `first-row` makes the table a data table with its header row again. The PDF tags it `table/tagging=div`, in a group, behind `\ifdefined\tagpdfsetup`, a data table inside it as a table again (tagging on, and the two plugs the division setting changes set back to a table's), and its caption as a paragraph before it, which LaTeX doesn't number: a longtable's caption is in its head, which the division setting tagged a TH with no row, 10 of them in *Prealgebra 2e*'s third chapter, whose numbered step tables have captions ("Table 3.2"). Its id is an anchor there, since Pandoc's writer holds the id of a table without a caption in an empty one, in that head (Writers/LaTeX/Table.hs, 3.12): with the caption moved and the id left on the table, the chapter still had its 10 TH with no row, and LaTeX's "Table 3" above each numbered step table, under its own number. *Elementary Algebra 2e*'s step tables, classed `unnumbered`, get no empty caption from the writer, and no `\label` either, so their ids had no anchor in the PDF; each has one now. `build-pdf.py`'s caption matching skips a layout table, which the PDF has no Table for. A Markdown target writes it as HTML with its role, so it reads back as one. A `source` target writes a person's `layout` into an HTML page as its role and into a LaTeX book as its tagging, and says it can't into a Word file. A deck's tables don't take `layout`; the slides' remediation warns of a value it doesn't know, as it would of any. Each new check fails without its part, in 19 ways of breaking it, two found passing at first and given checks of their own (the page's marks as read, which every target got right anyway, since the filter makes every layout table `presentation` again; and the filter run on its own, with no pre-pass, which reads the page's role). Unpacked with this branch and converted whole (HTML and EPUB, validators on), *Prealgebra 2e*, *Elementary Algebra 2e*, and *Intermediate Algebra 2e* have 8, 0, and 8 `table-without-headers-or-caption` findings, page and EPUB each, where they had 816, 1,012, and 840 (`main` at a8acfe7, 2026-10-13); the 4 tables left in each of two books are classed `unnumbered`, property boxes and the like, not steps. Every layout table in the pages (854, 840, and 830; *Intermediate Algebra 2e*'s five one-cell pictures are figures) has no scroll region and every data table has one. The Nu checker's warnings are what they were (composing characters), epubcheck finds nothing, and *Prealgebra 2e*'s `table-header-is-formula` findings fall from 460 to 440, from step tables the census had given a header column. As PDFs (TeX Live 2026, no contents), *Elementary Algebra 2e*'s fifth chapter (11 pages, 5.2 among them, which stopped LaTeX before item 19) is 1,226 Divs and no Table, and has 51 more named destinations than without the anchors, one for each step table; *Prealgebra 2e*'s third, with its numbered step tables, has its 16 data tables as Tables and every TH in a row, each numbered step table's number a paragraph before it and no number of LaTeX's beside it. The output check finds in each what it did before the anchors (4 `pdf-table-header-is-formula` in the third chapter's data tables). veraPDF 1.30.3, installed from software.verapdf.org (I had looked for it on Maven Central, which refuses this container), passes the PDF suite's notes book, its layout tables among them, one with a head (PDF/UA-2 with Tagged PDF, and both WTPDF 1.0 profiles), and the suites' nine other checks of veraPDF's pass too. So does *Prealgebra 2e*'s third chapter, unpacked with this branch and built as a PDF with the five (2026-10-16, TeX Live 2026, no contents): 127 pages, its 16 data tables Tables and its step tables Divs, in which veraPDF finds nothing, PDF/UA-2 or either WTPDF profile. Not tried: a screen reader.
21. **A Word table is found as the header pre-pass reads its first cell.** The filter finds each table the pre-pass listed by its shape and its first cell's first 20 bytes, and it read the cell otherwise than the pre-pass does: a no-break space as other than a space, Lua's `%s` being ASCII's; a cell's paragraphs run together, as `stringify` gives them; and a formula as its TeX, where the pre-pass has its letters (`x_{n}` and `xn`). Such a table took no declaration, the guess's or a row of `table-headers.csv`'s, `layout` included, and lost the bookmarks before it, which links to it go to: seven tables in the slides session's nine books, each warned of twice as not matching. `cell_words` reads a cell as `tablecensus.cell_text` and the pre-pass's join do, its paragraphs apart and every space Python's `split()` takes for one a space, and compares only the words before a formula; a table that doesn't match is warned of once, the warning naming what isn't applied. The five of the seven in the seven books here (*Clinical Nursing Skills* 8.4's burn types, and four in *Principles of Data Science*, three opening with a formula) match now, and the links to four of them, which went nowhere on the page (`link-to-missing-fragment`) and in the EPUB (RSC-012), land. The convert suite's new case declares three Word tables `layout`, one for each kind of first cell; it fails without the change, and with each of its three parts undone.
22. **A link to a Word box of one cell lands on the bookmark before it.** OpenStax's export bookmarks every table between blocks, where Pandoc's reader drops a bookmark, and the pre-pass hands the filter those before a table, which it puts back as anchors. It did so only for a table it lists, which a box of one cell, or a table of pictures alone, which the census counts no table, isn't, though the filter's comment counted on a boxed note's, so a link to one went nowhere: *Clinical Nursing Skills*' link to its Table 25.5, a muscle-strength scale in one cell, on the page and in the EPUB, as the slides session found. `table-headers.py` hands the filter such a table's bookmarks too, with its shape and first cell to match it by, and still lists it nowhere. The convert suite's new case links to a Word box of one cell, and fails without the change.
23. **A PDF's table with an id and no caption isn't numbered** (TextbookImprover observations). Pandoc's LaTeX writer holds such a table's id in an empty caption, `\caption{}\label{…}`, which LaTeX numbers, so "Table 1" stood over a table the book never numbered; and a table of class `unnumbered` gets no caption and so no `\label`, so a link to it went nowhere. `pdf-target.lua` makes the id an anchor before the table, as item 20 does a layout table's, and a figure holding only the table, which the run writes as the table with the figure's caption, gives the table back its own id. The PDF suite's notes book gets two such tables, the second `unnumbered`, each with a link to it; its new check fails without the change.
24. **A PDF's table longer than a line wraps its cells** (TextbookImprover observations). Pandoc's LaTeX writer sets a table whose columns have no widths, each of whose cells is one paragraph with no line break, or none, in `l`, `c`, or `r` columns, which don't wrap; an HTML page's table without a `<colgroup>` is one, as is every table the unpacker writes, which leaves OpenStax's column widths out. `pdf-target.lua`'s `fit_to_line` gives such a table wider than a line, reckoned in characters, widths: each column's in proportion to its longest cell, a cell across columns shared among them, and no narrower than its longest word, a formula counted as one word with its operators' spaces; the writer sets a table with widths in `p` columns, which wrap. A table whose longest words alone are wider than a line is left as it is. In *Prealgebra 2e*'s third chapter, 25 tables ran past the page's edge, by 6.7 to 258 points (LuaLaTeX's overfull alignments in its second pass), a worked example's steps most of them, Table 3.2 among them; with the change none does, and 62 of the chapter's 124 tables are given widths (the pages compared by eye). The PDF suite's notes book gets a layout table and a data table whose cells, side by side, are longer than a line; its new check, that each long cell is on more than one line of the PDF's text, fails without the change.

And one from Rob's run of *Principles of Marketing* (The exercise bank, below), `landmarks.mbox` on 64a1200, which Rob pushed (413e752, 2026-10-17), with an entry in PANDOC-NOTES on what texmath does with a forced line break and a word in an `mi`, which the run found too:

25. **A page's content is its main landmark, its header and footer around it.** WAVE said "No page regions" of the book's pages: most of an HTML target's pages had no landmark at all. Each page's content is in `<main>` now, the `header` setting in a `<header>` before it and the `footer` setting in a `<footer>` after it, and with `menu: on` the menu and the pager between them. They go in through `header-includes.lua`, before the page's own `include-before` and after its own `include-after`, since Pandoc's `--include-before-body` and `--include-after-body` replace those as `--include-in-header` replaces `header-includes`: a page's epigraph, given in its metadata, was lost on a target with a header. Notes that `notes.numbering` or `notes.placement` moves go at the end of `<main>`, where they had gone after the footer, and `page.css` keeps the banner looking as it did, where Pandoc's stylesheet centers every `<header>`. A review of the change by a separate agent found three more things, each fixed with a check: a source page's own main element that Pandoc's reader keeps whole (a `<main>` with an id or a class, as WordPress writes one, or an element with `role="main"`) was a second main landmark inside the page's, and read back, its text twice, so `html-source.lua` opens it up as it does a `<section>` (PANDOC-NOTES' `extractMain` entry was wrong about this, and is corrected); a header or footer setting that's a `<header>` or `<footer>` already was nested in another, and is used as it stands; and a reveal.js presentation's script took the place of a deck's own `include-after`, as the page's header had, and goes in after it now.

And one from building *Principles of Marketing* whole as a PDF (2026-10-17), in `followups.mbox`:

26. **A heading inside a list, a quotation, a table, a figure, or a footnote is a paragraph tagged as a heading in a PDF.** The whole book, built from its unpacked pages (311 pages, 1,184 s to the output check's end), had 27 sections tagged inside a list, which veraPDF's PDF/UA-2 and both WTPDF profiles reject (`L-Sect` 27 times, `LBody-LI` once): an item of 10.1's list of new products holds a "Link to Learning" note, its title and its video's title headings, which Pandoc's LaTeX writer makes `\paragraph{Link to Learning}` and `\subparagraph{Oculus Quest 2}` inside the `\item`; of the 16 books with Key Equations, *Calculus Volume 3* has such headings too, three "Media" notes in 7.3's list items. LaTeX's tagging can't close a section opened inside an environment ("The structure Sect can not be closed"), so the list's structure took in the sections after it. Measured in LaTeX alone, a sectioning command does that inside an `itemize`, an `enumerate`, a `description`, a `quote`, a `longtable`'s cell, a `figure`, and a `\footnote`. `pdf-target.lua` sets such a heading as a paragraph tagged as the heading it is (`\tagpdfsetup{para/tag=H5}`, `H1` to `H6` by its level, as LaTeX's tagging tags the sections), in bold, kept with what follows (`\nobreak`; longtable makes `\nopagebreak` its own, which stopped LaTeX in a cell), its id an anchor a link goes to; in a footnote, which may hold no heading (veraPDF's `FENote-Hn`, found on the way), a bold paragraph. Such a heading isn't in the contents or the bookmarks, as a section is. The PDF suite's notes book gets a page with a heading in each of the seven, a link to one, and a section after them; `main`'s filter fails veraPDF on it with seven rules in each profile, and each new check fails without its part of the change (11 mutations). With the change, the book's PDF (1,128 s) has no list holding a section, the note's headings are `H5` and `H6` in the item, and veraPDF fails one rule in each profile: 8.4.5.9, the `.notdef` of two of the three emoji in a link's text in 2.2 (🌎 and 📚, in a video's title), which no font the build falls back to has, and which the run warns of. Built as PDF/UA-1, a page with such headings passes too, its levels unskipped.

And one from Rob's answer on the `mi` (2026-10-18), in `for-main.mbox`:

27. **A word or a unit in an HTML source's formula is text, when it's more than two letters.** Rob, of the open question: "would a rule like 'only turn into `mtext` when more than two letters' work well? If so, let's do that." Pandoc's MathML reader splits an `mi` of more than one letter into italic letters, unless it's a name texmath knows (PANDOC-NOTES), where MathML sets it whole and upright, as OpenStax's pages show it. `htmlrepair.words_as_text` makes an `mi` of more than two letters, and of letters and spaces alone, an `mtext` before Pandoc reads a page, so in `main` and for every HTML source, the unpacker's pages among them, and `convert.py` does the same to what a MathJax rendering gives up; one of two letters, a name texmath keeps (`sin`, `lim`), one letter repeated, and one styled other than normal or bold are left as they were. In the 16 books with Key Equations and *Marketing*: 1,528 identifiers of 191 kinds in 1,130 formulas, each a unit (`MeV` 206, `mol` 173, `rad` 136, `atm` 91), a name texmath doesn't know (`cis` 174, the Polish `arctg` 19), a word (*Marketing*'s `Revenue`, the Polish `lat` and `rok`), or a label (`net`, the y-intercept's `int`). The one product the rule took was *College Algebra with Corequisite Support 2e*'s x⁵ written out as `xxxxx` (over `xx`, in 6.5), which is why a repeated letter is left. A bold `mi` is `\textbf{}`, upright as the source sets it, where texmath made each letter bold italic (*Marketing* 18.3's "Total net sales"); an italic one would be `\textit{}`, and a bold italic one `\textit{\textbf{}}`, which texmath can't read back (PANDOC-NOTES), so those stay. The 4,387 of two letters are as they were (open questions). The unit suite checks each part of the rule, and the convert suite a page's own formula and one MathJax drew, on pages of their own; each check fails without its part of the change (14 mutations). Nine of the formulas before and after, as the HTML target writes them, went to Rob as `formula-words.html`.

And one found making the step table for Rob's screen reader (2026-10-18), in `for-main.mbox`:

28. **An unquoted `on` or `off` is the word, not a boolean.** YAML 1.1 reads an unquoted `on` and `off` as booleans, so `title_page: off` in the example's `conversion.yaml` stopped the run ("title_page is False, which is not one of auto, on, off"), and so did `menu: on`, as the docs write it. Where `on` or `off` is one of a setting's values, `oerconfig` takes a boolean as the word; a setting of other fixed values refuses one, saying to quote it, as a text setting already did. `merge`'s own `off` was the boolean in the schema, so the settings reference listed its values as `False` and `groups`; it's quoted now, as the other settings' are. Two fixtures in the configuration suite; each fails without its part of the change, and 13 of the 29 fail with the schema's `off` unquoted again.

And four from the CNX project's open items (2026-10-19), in `cnx-open.mbox`:

29. **The header guess takes a number signed with a minus sign for a number.** Rob: "Let's also fix 'the guess doesn't treat a real minus sign (U+2212) as a minus'." `NUMERIC` in `lib/tablecensus.py`, the pattern a cell must match to be a number, took a hyphen for a negative's sign and not the minus sign, which OpenStax's books write one with, as `QUANTITY` and `AMOUNT` already did; `parse_number` reads the sign, so a column of such numbers is ordered by it. Measured with the census on the Word corpus's seven books (2,943 tables), no guess changes, with an en dash taken too or not; on the 67 books here (12,221 tables), one: *Contemporary Mathematics*' 3.2, a table of numbers (−3, 24, 36/4, …, −16.0) beside whether each is an integer, whose first column the guess took for labels (`first-column`), two of its numbers being words to it, and takes for numbers now (`unknown`): the first guess is the one a person would make, but it was made for a reason that was wrong. No decision of the unpacker's changes. An en dash for the sign, as some of the math books write one (`x | –4.1 | –4.01 | …`), would change 14 guesses more, 7 `both` to `first-row` and 5 `first-column` to `none` among them; it isn't taken, since Rob asked for the minus sign, and an en dash is a range's too. The census suite's three cases with the minus sign; each fails without its part of the change.

30. **An identifier of two letters is text when the source sets it upright or applies it as a function** (item 27's rule widened). Item 27 left an `mi` of two letters alone, as likely a product (`dx`) as a unit (`cm`). One the source styles `mathvariant="normal"` is upright by its author's choice, as a unit, an element's symbol, or a word is and a product of variables isn't (MathML sets an `mi` of several letters upright anyway, which texmath doesn't, so the attribute is said on purpose); one before U+2061, MathML's function application, is a function's name. `htmlrepair.words_as_text` makes both an `mtext`, one letter twice (`mm`) among them. In OpenStax's 49 public repositories, their CNXML read with the rule: 4,154 set upright and 17 applied (the Polish `tg`), of 101 kinds, all in *Fizyka dla szkół wyższych* but 14, `cm` 966, `nm` 502, `kg` 493, `eV` 343, `mm` 233, `km` 163, `µF` 132, elements' symbols (`He` 58, `Sr`, `Th`), and `zł` 27 the most; the 14 are *Contemporary Mathematics*' "or" and "in", blood types (`AB`, `Rh`), and `cm`, and two names *Calculus* splits (`si` before `n″`, `co` before `s`). No product is among them, and no formula of theirs carries a TeX annotation Pandoc would read instead. The other 988 of two letters are as they were: products most (`πr`, `rω`, `ρv`, `mc` in *College Physics*), words (`of`, `or`), abbreviations (`FV`, `PV`), and units nothing marks (open questions). Three unit checks; each fails under its mutations (9).

31. **A table of one cell with no caption is a layout table whatever its source.** The unpacker lays one out (Design decisions), and the census counts one no table (`layout(single-cell)`), but `figures-and-tables.lua` treated one from any other source as a data table: a Word file's box around a note was asked a caption, warned of no header row, and wrapped in a scroll region. `is_layout` takes a table of one cell for a layout table where no row of `table-headers.csv` decides, unless its source makes the cell a header cell, a page's `<th>` (its marker), a LaTeX longtable's head or a Markdown table's header row (the head Pandoc reads), or a Word table's row marked to repeat as a header, or a caption presents it as a table: its own, or a label paragraph beside it that a data table would take ("Table 25.5"). The head Pandoc's Word reader makes of a box's row when Word's table look formats it, as Word formats every table it inserts, isn't a declaration, so the header pre-pass hands the filter such a box, unlisted, with `look_head`. The first version laid out a box whatever its head and caption, and the full run of the suites before delivery found what that cost: the LaTeX target's table of one header cell, read back, laid out where the page it came from had it a data table (the LaTeX suite's round trip); a LaTeX book's captioned tables of one cell laid out (its check of floats); and the convert suite's list in a table of one cell written as HTML by a Markdown target, as any layout table is (its table has a header row now, so its list is written in Markdown as before). Laid out, a captioned table loses the number LaTeX gives its caption: a Markdown page's table of one cell captioned "Grades." was set in its PDF as "Grades." where the text says "See Table 1.", and is "Table 1: Grades." again. The Word corpus's files, which Pandoc wrote, have 2 tables of one cell among their 2,943, neither with a table look: a figure's label beside its picture in one cell in *Principles of Data Science*'s chapter 5, a layout table now, and *Clinical Nursing Skills*' Table 25.5, a muscle-strength scale in one cell (item 22's box), which the label paragraph after it captions, a data table as on `main`. Converted from the corpus's files, which have no pictures, 4 boxes of pictures alone come out as empty layout tables as well; with their pictures they're figures, as before. The convert suite's Word boxes, one Word's table look heads and one whose row is marked to repeat, an HTML page's box, a header cell alone, a box with a caption of its own and one a label names, and two cells, the filter run alone too; nine mutations, each caught.

32. **The output check finds a formula Pandoc kept as its text** (`formula-kept-as-text`). Pandoc's HTML reader keeps a formula whose MathML texmath can't read, a script or a fraction of the wrong number of parts or an element it doesn't know, as its text in a span of class `math`, its parts run together, and says nothing of it, even with `--verbose` (PANDOC-NOTES); the check's `formula-shown-as-tex` looks for the TeX the writer falls back to, which this isn't, so the runs of *Fizyka dla szkół wyższych* said nothing of the 20 formulas whose scripts the unpacker now repairs (Design decisions). The check names each such span by its text, on a page and in an EPUB, and leaves a page with a script alone, as `formula-shown-as-tex` does; a span of class `math` with others, as Sphinx writes MathJax's TeX in, isn't one. Every page of *Fizyka*'s first two volumes put through Pandoc (511) gives 20 findings on 12 pages unpacked without the repair, those 20 formulas, and none with it; *Introduction to Sociology 3e*'s pages and EPUB, converted whole with this series, and the 311 pages of *Principles of Marketing* converted here on 2026-10-17, none. An HTML source's malformed MathML, or a kind no one has met yet, is reported now, where it went by unsaid. The check suite's page that breaks every check, a page with a script, and a page and an EPUB Pandoc wrote from formulas texmath can't read; six mutations, each caught.

And two from Rob's choice for the blanks (2026-10-19), in `blanks.mbox`:

33. **A PDF's fill-in blank that runs past its line is drawn to end where the line does.** Rob, of the blanks in Sociology's quiz: "change it to fill the rest of the line when it will overrun into the margin and leave it alone otherwise. Breaking onto the next line would probably not achieve what the original author intended." TeX can't break a run of underscores, so a question ending in a blank too long for what's left of its line ran into the margin: two in *Introduction to Sociology 3e*'s chapter 19 section quiz, "The Rating of Perceived Exertion (RPE) is an example of" and its blank by 24.3 points, and "Many deaths in high-income nations are linked to" and its blank by 5.7 (TextbookImprover observations). `pdf-target.lua` marks a run of four or more underscores in the text (`\OERBlank`), but not in code, a link's text, a picture's description, a heading, or the metadata, whose plain text Pandoc's writer takes too (a bare address's `\url`, `alt=`, a bookmark's `\texorpdfstring`, `title-meta`; PANDOC-NOTES), nor what lua-ul underlines, strikes through, or highlights (`\ul`, `\st`, `\hl`), which it draws as the line is made, before a blank could be drawn shorter; and `latexbuild.PDF_MACROS` gives LuaLaTeX a function that runs once a paragraph's lines are made (`post_linebreak_filter`): on a line wider than the text, a marked run that reaches past its end is drawn shorter, its underscores overlapping by kerns, by as much as it takes for the line to end at the margin with its spaces at their natural width, and no shorter than three underscores. Every underscore is still in the PDF's text, and a blank that fits, or one on a line too wide for something else that doesn't reach the line's end, is left as it is. TeX has reported the line as too wide by then, so the function names the line in the log, as wide as TeX said and at the line of LaTeX TeX said, and how wide it is now, and `overfull_lines` counts it as it is now. The quiz built alone: the two lines end at the margin, their spaces 3.32 points where TeX had shrunk them to 2.21, every other word of the PDF where it was, the tagged text's 16 and 18 underscores all there, and veraPDF passing it. The whole book, built again with this series (1,109 s, two cores): of every glyph on its 953 pages, only the two quiz lines' moved, their text the same, each line now ending 0.2 points past the margin, the last underscore's own overhang, where it had run 24.3 and 5.7 points past; the tagged text the same; the log naming the two lines as fitted; and veraPDF passing it (PDF/UA-2 and both WTPDF profiles). The PDF suite's book (a blank that runs past its line, one that fits, and underscores in code, a bare address, a picture's description, and a heading) and its notes book (a blank on a line too wide that doesn't reach its end, one with too little room before the margin, drawn three underscores wide, and blanks underlined, struck out, and highlighted, unmarked), with `tests/pdftext.py` reading where each glyph is drawn; and unit checks of the count and of the Lua, which TeX reads before Lua does, so it's written without a percent sign, a hash, a tilde, a backslash, or a comment. Each new check fails without its part of the change, in 16 ways of breaking it; a 17th, the kerns without their underscores' attributes, which the first version copied to them, broke nothing, since LaTeX's tagging passes over a kern, and the kerns carry none now.

34. **A line too wide in the contents is named as the contents'.** TeX reports a line too wide at a line of the file it's reading, which, in the contents or in a list of figures or tables, is the list's own (`book.toc`), and the build had looked it up in the book's LaTeX: of the 20 lines its warning named on Sociology's chapter 19 quiz, 18 were the contents' page numbers for the preface's sections (TextbookImprover observations), counted as lines of text, on no page. `PDF_MACROS` has LaTeX say in the log where it sets each list (`\@starttoc`, which the classes' lists go through), and `overfull_lines` gives a line between those marks the kind contents, or list for a list of figures or tables, on no page; the warning says "in the contents" or "in a list of figures or tables". Built again, Sociology's warning says "18 in the contents; the widest is in the contents, 9 points too wide", and names no line of text. The notes book gets a heading too long for a line of its contents and a caption too long for one of its list of figures, and the unit checks a log with both. Three mutations, each caught.

And one found measuring OpenStax's page (2026-10-19), in `contents-depth.mbox`:

35. **A PDF's contents and bookmarks go as deep as `pdf.toc_depth` says.** The setting counts levels as the EPUB's contents do, an entry at the top of `project.contents` being level 1, and `build-pdf.py` handed it as it stood to Pandoc's template, which sets LaTeX's `tocdepth` with it, and LaTeX counts a chapter as 0; Pandoc's writer takes one off its own `--toc-depth` for a class with chapters (`Writers/LaTeX.hs`), but not off a `toc-depth` in the metadata, as the build set it. So at the default, 2, a book's contents and bookmarks took in its pages' subsections too: *Introduction to Sociology 3e*'s were 1,030 entries, the contents on 24 pages, where its EPUB's contents have the 295 of two levels, and the preface began on page xxvii, so the contents' page numbers for its sections were too wide for their column (item 34; TextbookImprover observations). A class with chapters takes one less now; a `toc-depth` the `pdf.metadata` file sets is LaTeX's own count, as before, which the setting's description now says. Built again with the fix, Sociology's contents are 8 pages, its preface begins on page xi, no line of them is too wide, and the book is 937 pages where it was 953. The PDF suite's book checks the contents' and the bookmarks' depth, a page's sections in and a subsection out, which fails without the fix.

And two from Rob's choices for OpenStax's page (2026-10-19, late), in `openstax-page.mbox`:

36. **The `footer` setting is on every page of every format.** It had been an HTML target's alone, though the unpacker's comment said every target's, so the PDF and the EPUB of an unpacked book lacked the attribution OpenStax's license page asks every page of a copy to carry (TextbookImprover observations). Rob: "Yes, we should be adding the footer to every output format. In general, it should be settable per target, and we should set it for every target when making OpenStax books." An EPUB's content documents end with it, after the notes a chapter's file ends with, but not its cover or its navigation document (`build-epub.py`'s template, `navpage` and `coverpage`; PANDOC-NOTES); a PDF's every page has it at the foot, the title page and each chapter's first among them, centered in small type under whatever the page style puts there, single-spaced whatever the text's spacing, an artifact as a page number is (`lib/pagefooter.py`'s patch to every page style); a Word file has it in a footer part of its own (`docxtarget.add_footer`); a Markdown or AsciiDoc file's text ends with it, in an `oer-footer` div or open block written as the rest of the file is (`target-blocks.lua` adds it before the target's filters run), which reading the file back takes out (`figures-and-tables.lua`). Outside an HTML page a picture is its alternative text and a link to one of the book's own files its words, since an EPUB carries only the files its chapters name and a target's folder only those its pages do; a heading is a paragraph, which no contents or bookmarks take in; an id is left out; and any link is its words in a PDF's foot and a Word footer. A page style made from another, as fancyhdr's `\fancypagestyle{plain}[fancy]` makes one, has its foot wrapped once (wrapped again, the foot called itself until TeX ran out of memory), and a foot set inside another, as fancyhdr's odd foot gives its even one when fancyhdr is twoside and the class isn't, isn't wrapped again. Each part fails a check of the convert, unit, or PDF suite without it. On *Introduction to Sociology 3e*: all 296 content documents of its EPUB end with it, all 243 of its Word files have it, and each of the PDF's 967 pages has it once; the output check, epubcheck, and veraPDF find nothing.

37. **An OpenStax book's PDF has OpenStax's paper and margins, with 12-point type.** Rob: "I agree with the strategy of matching their margins but not their smaller typeface with many more characters per line. … If there are other formatting adjustments we can copy that will improve things, then we should consider those. The unpacker also seems like a logical place to do it." `unpack-cnx.py` writes `pdf-layout.yaml` beside `conversion.yaml`, which names it as `pdf.metadata`: Letter, or A4 for `pl-nursing` and `pl-u-physics`, the carnival design's Polish books, which alone set a size; 1 inch at the sides and 0.8 at the top and bottom; the book class's own type at 12 points, not OpenStax's 9; lines 1.5 times the type's size apart (`linestretch: 1.25`), as OpenStax's are; ragged right, as OpenStax's text is, with LaTeX's own `\raggedright` at `\AtBeginDocument`, which breaks no word at a line's end (ragged2e's `\RaggedRight` hyphenates, and an earlier sample here used it); and the page number 0.3 inch under the text (`footskip`), so the footer's line under it is a third of an inch from the paper's edge, where OpenStax's is about 0.37, and not within a quarter inch, as the class's 30 points would put it. The footer is OpenStax's words without a link (Rob: "if it doesn't violate their requested attribution clause, let's leave it unlinked"), "Access for free at openstax.org.", as the license page gives them and OpenStax's PDFs print them. Measured on *Introduction to Sociology 3e*: 967 pages, chapters 1 to 20 on 884, where the target's page took 937 and 858 and OpenStax's PDF takes 664 and 618; 89 characters to a full line; no line wider than the text; veraPDF passes. At the class's own line spacing (14.5 points), 828 pages. *Introductory Business Statistics 2e*: 900 pages, where the target's page took 872 and OpenStax's PDF takes 627, and 54 lines wider than the text on 6 pages (52 in tables, the appendix's statistical tables the widest, and 2 display formulas), where the target's page had 105 on 23; veraPDF passes. Checked in the CNX suite (the files, the A4 styles, the LaTeX a converted book's `latex` target writes) and the PDF suite's footer book, built in this layout (the page, the type, the spacing, the rag, the footer's place), each failing without its part. Item 38 made this page the PDF target's default, the unpacker's file a copy of it, and its ragged right no longer `\raggedright`, which made a line break's two lines two paragraphs in the PDF's structure.

38. **OpenStax's page is the PDF target's default for every book, in `bin/pdf-layout.yaml`.** Rob: "Let's adopt these new settings as our default and make sure they are clearly documented and easy to configure. E.g., let's have the default YAML settings available where they are easy to change or copy. Let's also advise that type should be increased to 13.5 pt or margins adjusted to 1.3 in if meeting WCAG 1.4.8 (AAA) is the goal, and mention the tradeoffs." The file holds item 37's page, fontsize, papersize, geometry, linestretch, and `ragged-right`, with a comment on each and, last, the three lines WCAG 1.4.8 would take; `build-pdf.py` lays it under a book's `pdf.metadata` file, which changes it a field at a time; the configuration page shows it whole (a unit check keeps the two the same), with the four lines that give the class's own page back. A LaTeX book's PDF from its pages keeps the book's own layout, and the class's page where its preamble gives none (`convert.py` passes even an empty layout on). The unpacker's `pdf-layout.yaml` is a copy of the file, its opening comment saying where it came from, on A4 for the two Polish styles; it writes none where one is already, as before, so **a book unpacked since 63d7756 keeps its old file**, whose `\raggedright` splits a paragraph at each line break: delete it and unpack again, or copy `bin/pdf-layout.yaml` over it. A type size or a paper a metadata file gives as a class option replaces the default's `fontsize` or `papersize`: the book class takes its options in the order it declares them, so the default's 12pt had won over a file's `classoption: [11pt]` (PANDOC-NOTES); a size the class hasn't, given so, is the book's fontsize. And margins or line spacing the file's own `header-includes` set, loading geometry or setspace, keep the default's `geometry` or `linestretch` out: a book written for Pandoc's page that loaded geometry itself had stopped LaTeX ("Option clash for package geometry"), found by a separate agent's second review of the change. Ragged right is now the right margin given all the stretch, as `\raggedright` gives it, in footnotes, captions, and minipages too (each sets its lines justified again, `\@parboxrestore`), and not `\raggedright` itself, whose `\\` ends the paragraph: LaTeX's tagging made a line break's two lines two paragraphs, and added to `\@parboxrestore` it stopped *Introductory Business Statistics 2e*'s build on Figure 13.8's caption, two lines with a break between them, which LaTeX sets in a box first ("Not allowed in LR mode"). Ragged text is set without microtype's font expansion, which a ragged line doesn't need and which drew one's letters up to 2% narrower to fit more on it (in the PDF suite's footer book, a caption's 11.75 points of 11.96, a footnote's 9.77 of 9.96). A type size the book class hasn't is set by KOMA-Script's scrextend, ahead of the author's packages, its check of `\@footnotemark`, which LaTeX's tagging changes, skipped (it warned on every build); the class had ignored `13.5pt` and set 10. What scrextend leaves at the class's 10pt is set again as the class sets it at its own sizes, a list's indents and label's space (2.5em, which had been 25 points at 13.5pt), the space above a page's first line (`\topskip`, the type's size), and an indented paragraph's indent; and so are parskip's space between paragraphs and its lists, which scrextend replaced with its own, giving a list 14 points more above and below it than a paragraph (PANDOC-NOTES); found by a separate agent's first review of the change. The contents leave room for a page number of four digits, which a book on this page can reach: in 13.5-point type, 62 lines of *Sociology*'s contents ran into the margin. Measured on the whole books, each built from the same pages: *Introduction to Sociology 3e* is 977 pages on the default page, 1,160 at 13.5 points, and 1,040 with 1.35-inch side margins, its full lines averaging 88.4, 78.3, and 78.8 characters, spaces counted; *Introductory Business Statistics 2e* is 916, 1,062, and 959 pages, with 54, 81, and 81 lines wider than the text. Rob's 1.3 inches comes to just over 80, 80.4 in *Sociology*'s first four chapters and 80.3 in *Business Statistics*', so the docs give 1.35. Paragraphs 1em apart, which 1.4.8 also asks for, make *Sociology* 1,021 pages. Checked in the PDF suite (the footer book on the default page, a footnote's and a caption's rag, a line break in each, the letters at their size; a sized book of 13.5-point type, justified, its paragraphs' and a list's spacing, the list's indent, a page's first line, and a four-digit page number in its contents; the fancy book's 13.5pt, a size the class hasn't, and A4, as class options; the suite's book again on the default page, veraPDF finding there what it finds in the book; a book whose metadata file loads geometry and setspace itself; and the TeX packages the suite's books need, named when missing), the LaTeX suite (a LaTeX book that gives no layout keeps the class's page), the CNX suite (the copy, an edited default copied whole), and the unit suite (the configuration page's copy of the file, and the installation page's packages), each failing without its part.

39. **A box's rule above it goes to the next page with a heading that opens the box.** Found making OpenStax's notes boxes for Rob to see (TextbookImprover observations): a box, a div of class `box`, is set off by a rule above it, kept with its first line, but a heading's own place to break a page before it (`\@startsection`'s `\addpenalty\@secpenalty`) came after the rule, so *Sociology*'s "Big Picture" box on Global Culture, its heading and picture too tall for the page, left its rule at the foot of the page before, in a build of its section 1.3 alone. The rule now leaves LaTeX as a heading does (`\@afterheading`), so a heading after it adds no break and no space of its own, as a heading after a heading doesn't. Below, that state ends with the box, since a box that begins no paragraph, a figure alone, had left the heading after it without its space above; and a paragraph that opens a box is indented where the book indents them, as before (the first review's two findings). Neither touches `\everypar` while a list's item, a quotation's, or a definition's label waits for the item's first paragraph, which an untagged build sets it beside from `\everypar`: a box that opened an item had stopped LaTeX ("perhaps a missing \item"), found by the second review. A legend sits a medium space under the rule, without a heading's space above it. LibreTexts' Examples open with their legends, so its books had it too. Checked in the PDF suite's sized book, whose box is too tall for the 60 points left of its page, and whose headings after a box of a figure and a box of a paragraph are as far below them, and in its untagged book, whose boxes open a list's items, a quotation, and a definition.

40. **A page for WCAG 1.4.8 (AAA), `bin/pdf-layout-wcag-aaa.yaml`.** Rob, of the advice: "I don't want to adopt the WCAG 2.2 AAA conformant settings as the default, but it would be useful to write an alternate example configuration for folks that do want them, so they could just adopt that instead of the defaults. (I would do that using 13.5 pt type rather than the 1.35 in margins, unless you know a reason we shouldn't.)" It's the default page whole, so it can be copied over `bin/pdf-layout.yaml` as well as into a book's folder, with four changes: `fontsize: 13.5pt`, lines averaging about 78 characters; `linestretch: 1.5`, lines space-and-a-half apart as W3C's explanation of the criterion counts it, from single spacing, "the default spacing for the font", which the book class makes 1.2 times the type's size, so 1.8 times it; `\setlength{\parskip}{1.2em plus 2pt}`, paragraphs 250% of single spacing apart, top to top, 3 times the type's size; and `\SetSinglespace{1.5}`, setspace's own setting for what it calls single spacing in footnotes and floats, so a footnote's lines and a caption's, in a book with a table, where the caption package sets them, are spaced as the text's, where the default sets them 1.2 times their type's size apart. W3C's technique C21 counts line spacing from the type's size instead, by which the default's lines are space-and-a-half already; the configuration page gives that reading's three lines too (13.5-point type, 1em between paragraphs, `\SetSinglespace{1.25}`). Measured on *Introduction to Sociology 3e*: 1,414 pages on the page, 1,214 by C21's count, 977 on the default, 78.3 characters to a full line in both. No reason was found against 13.5-point type over 1.35-inch margins: *Business Statistics*' tables overrun alike, 81 lines either way, and the type serves a reader with low vision too, where the margins cost fewer pages (1,040 against 1,160, at the default's spacing). The default's closing comment, which had listed three lines, names the file. A unit check keeps the file and the configuration page's copy alike, the file holding every field the default does, on its paper and margins; the PDF suite builds a book on it, 13.5pt type on lines 1.8 times its size apart, paragraphs 3 times it, and a footnote's and a caption's lines 1.8 times theirs, each check failing without its part (4 mutations).

41. **An OpenStax book's notes are boxes, each headed by its kind and its own title in one heading.** Rob, of the suggestions: "go ahead and make those notes into boxes. (I assume this would by done by the CNX unpacker on a book-by-book basis, right? Somewhere we should be documenting where the config for each book that we've created lives, so it can be changed or overridden if desired.)" And of the label: "Is there a tag we could use that wouldn't get interpreted as a heading, and which would still be accessible? If so, that makes sense to me. Otherwise, I like the idea of merging the label and the title into a single heading. Have a heading with only one heading below it doesn't make sense." There isn't one in every format. HTML has `<aside>`, which a screen reader announces as a landmark named by its label (`aria-labelledby`); PDF 2.0 has an Aside structure type, but its label could only be a paragraph, Title being for a document's or a chapter's title and Lbl for a label inside another element, a list item's number or a heading's (ISO 32000-2, 14.8.4); and Word has neither. A paragraph is read, but a screen reader's list of headings passes it over. `cnx.emit` gives a note the class `box`, which every format draws (a border on a page, in an EPUB, and in Word, a rule above and below in a PDF), but a note of class `reference`, as the business styles write each entry of a references page; `unpack-cnx.py --no-boxes` leaves them all unboxed. A note's label, the kind baking titles it with, and its own title, which baking made a heading a level below it, are one heading at the label's level: the label in a span of class `os-note-label`, a colon, and the title (no colon after a label that ends in punctuation of its own, "Who Knew?", "Disorders of the..."). A title that only repeats the label (*Physics*' "Teacher Support") leaves the label alone, its ids anchors in it, and one that opens with it ("Try It: The nth Term…" under "Try it", in *Algebra 1*) stands alone. A space baking leaves at the label's end, its divider's after a number (*Calculus*' theorems, "Theorem 2.1 ") or its string's (the statistics books' "Stats Lab "), is taken off, where it had stood before the colon in 287 headings, which a separate agent's review found; the punctuation test hadn't seen it, `text_of` evening out spaces. And a title's own id, which OpenStax's assembly writes and our baking doesn't, stays, an anchor in the heading, as an id the label held does. `unpack-cnx.py` says how many notes it headed each way. Unpacked again (2026-10-20), the 67 books' 16,034 pages hold 32,414 notes: 30,527 boxes and the 1,887 references (*Principles of Management*, *Organizational Behavior*, and *Introduction to Business 2e*); 7,484 notes had been headed by two headings and are headed by one, 6,835 merged, 583 by the label alone (579 of *Physics*' "Teacher Support"), and 66 by the title; 21,814 have one heading, as before, and 3,116 none (the references, 981 equation callouts, and the like); and no page differs outside its notes. `util/cnx-compare-word.py` reads the merged heading's colon away, as the Word files have the label and the title apart; a note headed by its kind alone or its title alone still differs from them by the kind's words, which lists one page more of *Foundations of Information Systems*' 132, its first section, "Careers in IS". Where each book's settings are, and how to change them, is a section of `docs/cnx-input.md` now, "A book's settings, and where to change them". Checked in the CNX suite (in a book baked as economics, a note's title merged, one repeating the label, one opening with it in either case, one opening with a longer word, a note inside one a level under its heading, the title in the note once and no id twice, the unpacker's counts, and a reference not a box; in one baked as organic chemistry, "Why This Chapter?" without a colon; `--no-boxes`; and, from baking's markup, a label that ends in its divider's space, and the title's id and the label's kept), each check failing without its part (16 mutations).

42. **An EPUB's SVG picture holds no id twice.** Rob, of *Algebra 1*'s picture (Open questions): "Give me more detail on this. What is the ID used for? Do we also need to fix references to the ID?" `Algebra-1_09_12_06_01.svg`, a graph exported from Desmos, holds each of its three expressions' shapes in a group, `<g id="sketch-99cb8af3">`, the same id three times; its other ids are its layers' (`expressions-99cb8af3`, `grid-99cb8af3`), once each, and nothing in the file refers to any of them, no `href="#…"` and no `url(#…)`. epubcheck rejects a picture that holds an id twice (RSC-005), as XML does; a page shows the picture through `<img>`, whose file the Nu checker doesn't read, so only the EPUB failed. `build-epub.py`'s `unique_svg_ids` renames each repeat in the EPUB's copy, once Pandoc has written it: the first keeps the id, so what a reference finds, the first element with it, is the same element, and a repeat gets a number after it, `-2` and on, that no id of the file has. So no reference needs fixing, and the source's picture and the pages' copy are as they were. An id is a start tag's id attribute, found past the file's comments, CDATA sections, processing instructions, and DOCTYPE, so words in a comment or in the picture's text that look like one are left alone; and a repeated id the picture's own stylesheet names (`#dot`) is left repeated, the run warning of it, since a selector styles every element with the id, and renaming one would unstyle it. A separate agent's review found what the first version, which looked for ` id="…"` anywhere in the file's bytes, did to each: an earlier draft's tag in a comment, a repeat of an id a `<use>` then lost, and a figure's text reading `<div id="nav">`, each renamed. The run says how many pictures and ids. *Algebra 1* unpacked and converted with it (HTML and EPUB, validators on, 2026-10-20): the run renamed the picture's 2 repeats, and epubcheck finds nothing in the EPUB. The EPUB suite's pictures hold an id three times, a name a repeat would take already another element's, an id once, a comment holding a tag with an id the file has, and text that reads like a tag with one; and a stylesheet naming one of two repeated ids. Its three checks fail under seven mutations.

43. **A character of the Symbol font is the one it stands for.** Rob, of the characters left as written (Open questions): "Yes, map them." Word, saving text set in Microsoft's Symbol font, can keep the font's codes, which it writes in Unicode's private use area at F000 and the code (U+F061 for the font's alpha, 0x61), and which no other font draws: a page or an EPUB showed a box or nothing, and a PDF left each out, saying so. `cnx.emit`'s `SYMBOL_FONT` gives each of the font's codes the character Adobe's mapping of the font gives it, where that's Unicode's own; where Adobe's is a private use character of its own, the one Unicode has since added (the pieces of a tall bracket, ⎛ and the rest, and the sans serif ®, ©, and ™, which are the same characters); and the horizontal line, 0xBE, which Word's users set for a dash, an em dash. `finish` makes them so in a page's text, its attributes (a picture's description), and its formulas, as it makes a C1 control the Windows-1252 character it stands for, and `cnx.project` in the summaries and cells `table-captions.csv` is drafted from. Each is a row of the report (`symbol-font-character`), its code, how many, and what it was made, a summary's naming its table, since a dingbat font, Wingdings say, writes its characters there too, which would be made wrong: the report says each was taken as the Symbol font's. A separate agent's review raised it, with Wingdings' bullets and ticks (U+F0A7 ▪ made ♣, U+F0FC ✓ made ⎫), and found the summaries unreported. OpenStax's repositories here have no dingbat's: each of the ten there is the Symbol font's by its sense, and six reach a book:*Life, Liberty*'s two em dashes, in a letter home of 1945 ("ailments—a sprained right ankle"); *Introductory Statistics 2e*'s alpha ("Since α > p"); and a minus sign in a calculator screen's description ("a = −173.513363") in it, in *Statistics*, and in its Spanish edition, *Introducción a la estadística*. The other four, alphas, are in Try It solutions baking leaves out. In the 21 Connexions archive books here, 660 are in three: 658 in a Vietnamese book on solar energy, *Năng lượng mặt trời*, every one a Greek letter (τ 178 times, δ 77, ω 45) or a relation or operator (≤, ≈, ∑, ⊥, ⇒, and ÷, which it sets a range with, "(15÷30) km/s"), the Symbol font's by their sense; a minus sign in *Collaborative Statistics*; and in *Natural Sciences Grade 7*, 0x34 alone in a checklist's cell over its Yes and No columns, likely a dingbat's, made "4". The CNX suite's economics book has a paragraph with an alpha, a minus sign, and a dash, and a table's summary with the dash, which reaches the converted page's caption and the report, and `finish` is given an alt text's and a formula's; its checks fail under six mutations.

44. **A PDF's bookmarks go as deep as `pdf.toc_depth` says with no contents page too.** Rob, of the LibreTexts session's finding (Open questions): "Is this a bug in Pandoc? Can we work around it in a way that won't break when the bug is fixed?" Not by Pandoc's manual, which gives `toc-depth` as the "level of section to include in table of contents" and says nothing of bookmarks. But hyperref gives a heading a bookmark when its level is within LaTeX's `tocdepth` (its own `bookmarksdepth` unset), and Pandoc's template sets `tocdepth` only inside `$if(toc)$`, where it writes the contents, so a book whose `pdf.metadata` file says `toc: false` had the class's own depth, a subsection in the book class, and its bookmarks went a level deeper than `pdf.toc_depth` (item 35 had made the contents, and the bookmarks with them, as deep as the setting). `build-pdf.py` sets `tocdepth` at the head of `header-includes`, ahead of the author's own LaTeX, which can still set another; with a contents page the template sets it again, to the same. Were the template to set it outside `$if(toc)$`, it would set the same value, so the line would do nothing and break nothing. The PDF suite's preamble book has no contents page and a page with a section and a subsection, and its bookmarks are its pages and the section; the check fails with the line taken out (1 mutation).

45. **`build-pdf.py` run on its own: a relative `-d`, and a LaTeX book.** From PROJECT-NOTES' worth investigating; Rob, of each: "Fix it." With `-d book`, a folder given relative to where the build ran, and a `pdf.metadata` file, the build stopped ("Could not find metadata file"): Pandoc, which runs in the book's folder, was given the file's path relative to where the build ran. `metadata_file` gives it the whole path.
