In ragged lines, as the PDF target sets its text (`ragged-right`) and Pandoc's writer a table's cells, a blank longer than two thirds of its line is drawn at two thirds of it before TeX breaks the paragraph into lines, and a penalty of -51 (as `\linebreak[1]`) after it, or after a formula joined to it, asks TeX to end the line there; once the lines are made, it's drawn as long as its line leaves room for, up to its whole length. In ragged lines a line holding only a label is as good to TeX as any, its badness 0 (`\tracingparagraphs`), so TeX had broken the line after the label; in text set flush to both margins (`ragged-right: false`) such a line, or one ending at a blank drawn at two thirds, is too loose to take, and TeX keeps a label with a blank too long for its line itself, taking the line too wide as a last resort, which the blank is then drawn shorter to fit (a LaTeX file of its own, the label and a blank of 80 underscores on one overfull line), so the rule is for ragged lines alone. Item 57's offer, a no-break space before the blank, did worse, tried here: with no way to break a line without an overfull box, TeX takes the last break it was still considering (artificial demerits), which split a label of two words from its blank; and letting the blank shrink as glue gave its line a badness TeX's cubic measure made too high to take. In the fit book, two labels each with a blank of 72 underscores in a cell of the line's width (Namelabel, Datelabel): on `main` each label was alone on a line and its blank on the next; now each blank is on its label's line, drawn to end where it does, and the second label starts the next line. *Writing Guide with Handbook*'s 10.5, built alone in IBM Plex Serif: on `main`, Table 10.1's first blank is on a line of its own, "Name" alone on the line before, and the table, one cell too tall for what was left of page 21, is on page 22, page 21 two lines of text; with the change, "Name" and its blank share a line, and the table is on page 21, the section's PDF 25 pages, where it was 26. A blank drawn shorter, or drawn to fill its line, isn't hung into the margin: microtype hangs an underscore at a line's start or end a fifth of its width in Latin Modern (`mt-LatinModernRoman.cfg`, 1.76 points at 12), a tenth in a face it has no settings for (0.68 in IBM Plex Serif), and the margin's kern beside such a blank's underscore is set to nothing before it's drawn, a line with no room for that getting back as much of the kern as it needs; a blank that fits as TeX set it keeps its hang, as any character at a margin does, beside a blank drawn on its line too (item 62). LuaTeX doesn't always put that kern beside the underscore: after the penalty a paragraph's last line ends with, and before the node that opens a paragraph's first line (`local_par`), measured on that section, 22 of its 24 right kerns for an underscore and 12 of its 36 left ones; so the kern's underscore is found past the nodes of no width between them. Without that, the form's blanks that end a paragraph ran 0.7 points past their column, as on `main`. In the fit book, each blank drawn shorter ends within half a point of the margin, which the checks hold it to, where on `main` the blanks at a line's end ran 1.77 points past it, among them one that ends a paragraph (Endlabel), and one that opens one (Startafter) began 1.76 points into the left margin. `pdf-target.lua`'s widths count a blank as three underscores in a cell's longest word (`cell_size`), as the fit measures it, and whole in the cell's length; underscores the blank pass doesn't mark, in code, a link, an underline, a strikeout, or a mark, count whole: the table of two columns given half the line each that item 57's review proposed, a word of 26 letters in one and a blank of 64 underscores in the other (Halfblank), was set at 86 percent of its size on `main`, and is in the text's type. The counts give a worked example's steps a blank as three underscores now too (item 57's last open one). FIT_MACROS' measures leave out the kerns a blank drawn at two thirds overlaps by, which they meet in a cell's lines and a reason's paragraph, the blank's drawing running before them: in a cell, the blank is three underscores to the measure, and in a reason's width, whole (Joinlong, Wholereason). Each check fails under the mutation of its part, run on the fit book: the whole piece tested as the run alone (Joincell, and the build's report); the piece's end found at the first glue, inside a formula (Mathjoin, item 62); the two thirds left out (Namelabel, Datelabel, Endlabel, Mathjoin, item 62's Twoblank, Wholeback, and Shrunk, and the report); the penalty left out (Namelabel, Datelabel, Endlabel, Mathjoin, Wholeback, and a table's and the steps' blanks followed by a word, Halfblank and Blankreason, which then shared the blank's line); the two thirds in text set flush (Justname, item 62); the margin's kerns left alone (eight checks: Blankcell, Overblank, Halfblank, Edgy, Twoblank, Namelabel, Endlabel, and Blankreason); the kerns past the nodes of no width left alone (Endlabel, and Twoblank, whose drawn blank ends its paragraph); the margin's code as each review found it (item 62), before the first, every blank at a line's edge losing its hang and the line's spaces set again (Rightmargin, Leftmargin, Twoblank, Wholeback, Reachfit, and Shrunk), after it, a blank's kerns put back before it's drawn to fill its line (those and Edgy and Justname), and after the second, every hang on a line set to nothing where a blank is drawn (Twoblank, Wholeback, Reachfit, and Shrunk); of the third's fixes, a blank that ends whole not given its hang back (Wholeback), every hang on the line set to nothing and none given back (Twoblank, Wholeback), the blanks to draw measured with the hangs gone (Twoblank, Wholeback, Reachfit, and Shrunk) or at the line's natural spacing (Shrunk); the kerns counted, in a cell (Joinlong, and the build's report) and in a reason (Wholereason, and item 61's Rowblank, which the same measure lays out); the count of a blank whole (Halfblank, Edgy, Rowblank, which then has nothing to wrap by the count, and the report); and the count of code's underscores as a blank (Codeblank, and the report, item 62).

61. **A table without widths laid out by LaTeX's measure.** Rob, of item 52's offer of `formula_width` for every table's formulas (2026-10-23): "Yes, try the `formula_width` fix. Also have try having LaTeX measure everything and, if both get acceptable results, go with the faster approach." Both tried on *College Algebra 2e*'s chapter 3 and *Precalculus 2e*'s chapter 4, in Noto Sans, each a debug tree writing the fit's decisions to a file, one build at a time: `main`'s counts (base), `cell_size` counting a formula with `formula_width` (F), and LaTeX laying out every table whose source gives no widths, as item 56 lays out a worked example's steps, each column one of words (L). F broke formulas over lines that L kept whole: *College Algebra 2e*'s section 3.7, Table 2, the toolkit functions, ∛x after its equals sign (base broke √x too); and in *Precalculus 2e*'s Key Equations, "f(x) = ab^{x+c} + d" after its equals sign, which base had whole, and "f(x) = a log_b(x+c) + d", which base broke too; and in its section 4.4, Table 2's labels "f(x) = 2^x" and "g(x) = log_2(x)" beside pairs of numbers with fractions (base too). L broke none of them, set a table in the text's type that base and F set smaller (*Precalculus 2e*'s table of a graduate's debt by month, at 91 percent of its size in base and F, its label "Debt ($)" on two lines), and narrowed two tables' spaces where base narrowed four. So F's results aren't acceptable and L's are, and L is taken. It's the slower: in that round, one build at a time, base, F, and L took 373, 378, and 394 seconds for *College Algebra 2e*'s chapter and 443, 458, and 494 for *Precalculus 2e*'s; L as it ships (below), which measures fewer tables, took 393 and 451 seconds against the counts' 379 and 451, the counts with this round's other changes, in a second round run L first, and 275 against 270 for *Prealgebra 2e*'s chapter 3. As it ships, L laid out the two chapters as the prototype did, line for line (6,551 lines of text on 236 pages, and 7,360 on 219), measuring 24 of *College Algebra 2e*'s tables where the prototype measured 68. It differs from the prototype in three ways: the counts still lay the table out first (`fit_to_line`), giving LaTeX's first run its widths and a TeX without Lua its layout, where the prototype gave each column its count, or its count's share of the line where they're more together; a table with nothing to wrap by the count (each cell a word, a number, or a formula TeX can't break, `cell_size`'s length its longest word) keeps the writer's columns and the counts' fit, since a measure would set its columns as the writer does, and measured, a table of formulas in smaller type ran 3.5 points past its column on the third run (the fit book's Mathcells; the forms unicode-math sets small math in aren't as much smaller as the type); and a formula in a table's words is kept whole where the table fits its line so, and broken where TeX breaks a formula in text where it doesn't, before the table's type is set smaller (the measure keeps a formula's longest part between two of its breaks beside its whole width, which in steps, whose formulas don't break, are the same). A table is laid out so where its source gives no widths, every cell is a paragraph or none, none is across columns or rows, none holds a picture, and it isn't in a table's cell. Checked in the fit book, each failing without its part: a row's label with its formula beside five pairs with fractions on one line (Wholerow), which the counts broke over three; a sum too long for the line in its column, the table in its type (Piecerow), which with the formula kept whole was set at half its size and still ran past the paper's edges; a table of numbers and one of formulas a little too wide still fitted as before (Overstep, Mathcells), and the build's report of what's fitted as it was; and a blank too long for the line in a table whose columns the writer doesn't wrap (Rowblank), at 89 percent of its size on `main`, in the text's type, the blank drawn shorter. *Prealgebra 2e*'s chapter 3, whose 25 tables ran past the page's edge before its tables without widths were counted (item 24): 55 of its tables are laid out by the measure and 67 by the counts' widths, none narrowed or set smaller, as with the counts, and no line runs past the text's edge but by its last space; 18 have their cells' lines broken elsewhere than the counts broke them, as "Subtracting −4 from −7 is the same as adding 4 to −7" on one line where the counts broke it over two, and the chapter is 143 pages either way.

62. **What reviews of items 58 to 61's code found, and the fixes.** A separate agent read the round's code, ran no build, and named seven problems, each checked here, each real, and each fixed, its check failing under the mutation that undoes its fix. **Two blanks of 60 underscores**: the steps' check (Blankreason) found its blank as the first line holding 60 underscores, which item 60's table of two halves (Halfblank) put first, and a blank of 72 or 80 holds too; that table's blank has 64 now, and each check finds its run by a count no other case has or by its label's line, and holds it to within half a point of the margin, as the testing page says, where the checks had held it only to end within it (with the margin's kerns left alone, the steps' blank ends 1.76 points past the margin, which its check fails now). **A blank's margin kern set to nothing, then the line's spaces set again**: a line TeX had shrunk to fit, opening with a blank, ran past the margin, and a blank that fitted at a line's end was drawn shorter by the space TeX had taken from the line as well as by its hang (a paragraph of two lines whose spaces shrink to fit them, Rightmargin and Leftmargin: the blank at the first line's end had been drawn 39.9 points of its 43.9, and the second line ran 1.3 points past the margin). The first fix drew such a blank shorter by its kern, the line otherwise as TeX set it; a second review, of the fixes (an agent's again, which ran the blank code's Lua against a mock of LuaTeX's nodes), found that it put a kern back before a blank drawn at two thirds was drawn to fill its line, so that a blank alone in a narrow column hung past it, and the PDF suite's book of blanks failed two of its checks of a blank left as it is, which the fit book's checks hadn't. So only a blank drawn shorter, or drawn to fill its line, loses its hang now, its kern set to nothing before it's drawn and put back as far as a line with no room needs, and a blank that fits as TeX set it is left alone, hang and all, as any character at a margin is: Rightmargin's blank whole, Leftmargin's line ending at the margin, and a blank alone on a middle line of a column a tenth of the line wide (Edgy) from its column's left edge to its right, where the first fix's code hung it 1.8 points past each (on `main`, which widens its column for the blank counted whole, it began 1.8 points before its column and ended 0.6 past it). A table's cell opens its first line with the array package's strut, a rule, and ends its last with one, and LuaTeX hangs only a character at a line's end, so a blank alone in its cell has no kern at the margin: Edgy's blank, first alone in its cell, passed its check with the first fix's code and with the kerns left alone, which this round's mutations found, and it's on a middle line of its cell now. Leftmargin's check, written for the first fix, had asked its line to begin within the margin too, which the line, its blank hung, doesn't now; the PDF suite found it, and it asks the line to end at the margin. A third review, of the code as the second left it (an agent's again, the Lua run against a mock of LuaTeX's nodes), found that `fit` set to nothing the kern beside every blank on a line where it drew one: a blank that fitted beside one drawn lost its hang (a paragraph opening with a blank of five underscores, its line ended by one of 50 drawn to fill it, Twoblank: the first began at the margin, where microtype hangs it 1.8 points into it now), and so did a blank drawn at two thirds that its line had room for whole (Wholeback, 37 underscores, which began at the margin too); and whether a blank reached past its line's end was measured with those kerns gone, so that a blank that fitted as TeX set the line was drawn shorter where what followed it ran past (Reachfit: a blank of ten ending a point short of the line's end after one of five that opens the line, a space after it taking the line 1.5 points past, was drawn 3.2 points shorter). Only the kerns beside a blank the code draws are set to nothing now, the blanks chosen as TeX set the line, hangs and all, and a blank that ends whole gets its hang back. It noted, too, that Rightmargin's check didn't hold where its blank ends, which it does now, the blank hung as microtype hangs it and no further. Its second look, at that code (the mock's cases again, and 60,000 lines made at random, which found no other fault), found the blanks to draw still chosen at the line's natural spacing on a line with a blank drawn at two thirds, which the drawing sets again: on a line TeX had shrunk to fit, a blank that fitted was chosen and drawn shorter (Shrunk: a blank of five ending a point short of the line's end, its space shrunk two points, beside one of 40 drawn at two thirds that no break may follow, was drawn 40.1 points of its 43.9, and the blank of 40 began at the margin). They're chosen as TeX set the line now, spaces and hangs and all, and drawn at its natural spacing; the drawn blank of 40, its line having no room without its hang, gets it back. Wholeback's check asks, too, that the words after its blank be on the next line, as the -51 asks, so that a change of face or size leaving the blank short of two thirds fails it. A last look, at the final code, its cases and 80,000 random lines more, found nothing. **The two-thirds rule in text set flush to both margins** (`ragged-right: false`): a line ending after a blank drawn at two thirds is too loose for TeX to take, so a blank that fitted its line with its label shared it with the words after it, drawn shorter; the rule applies in ragged lines only now (item 60; a paragraph of the fit book set flush in raw LaTeX, Justname: the next label and its blank had shared the first's line, the two blanks drawn 415 points of their 491). **The -51 penalty inside a formula**: it went before the first glue after the blank, which for a formula joined to the blank is the formula's own, where TeX broke it before its relation; the penalty, and the joined piece's end, are past the formula now (Mathjoin: TeX had ended the line after x, = 1 on the next). **`cell_size` counted every run of four underscores as a blank**, code's too, which LaTeX doesn't draw shorter (`print(________)` in a column a tenth of the line, Codeblank: its column given 10 characters for its 15, the table set at 75 percent of its size); it counts what the blank pass marks, and only in a cell's longest word, since counting a blank as three underscores in the cell's length too, as this fix first did, left item 61's table of a blank too long for its line (Rowblank) nothing to wrap, set at 89 percent again. **An .aux file from an earlier build**: a `latex` target's folder keeps one, and `main`'s has a step's width as `\OERFitStepsState{id}{width}`, which the macro's new third argument read with the next line of the .aux for it, LaTeX ending with Lua's error (`unexpected symbol near '\'`, tried in a LaTeX file of its own); the width is `\OERFitStepsWidth` now, and the old name reads as nothing, the LaTeX suite building its latex target again beside an .aux so written, which fails with the old name's three arguments. **Checks that tested less than they said**: the blank checks (above); Piecerow's, which looked only at the line holding its label, and now every line of its table; and Twentytwo's table, which item 61 lays out by the measure now, its comment brought up to date, with a copy given a cell across two columns (Twentyspan), which keeps `fit_to_line`'s least widths checked, its corner's words wrapping (in the writer's columns, the corner's words on one line, it ran to within 5.2 points of the paper's edges at 53 percent of its size). The second review also found Mathjoin's check passing with no penalty at all, and Twentyspan's with its corner missing, each checked now. This round's mutations found two more parts no check failed without, FIT_MACROS' skipping of the kerns a blank drawn at two thirds overlaps by, in a cell's lines and in a reason's width, checked now (Joinlong, Wholereason, item 60).

63. **A worked example's step keeps its display style.** Rob, of the measurement in the open questions (2026-10-24): "Go ahead and implement the fix for display style". OpenStax sets some of a worked example's steps in display style, an `mstyle` with `displaystyle="true"` around a cell's math or a part of it, or the `mtable`'s own `displaystyle="true"` for all its cells, which Pandoc's reader drops (PANDOC-NOTES), so every output had set them in text style. The unpacker (`steptables._displayed`) finds a step's formula, a cell of math or a formula in a reason, that holds something display style draws otherwise, a fraction, a large operator such as an integral (by its `largeop`, or else as MathML's operator dictionary has it), or an operator's limits under or over it, a sum's or a limit's, set in display style as MathML sets it: inside an `mstyle` with `displaystyle="true"` nearer than one with `"false"`, or anywhere in the cells of a table that sets them so, the table of steps or a table in a cell, but not in a fraction's parts, a script, or the cells of a table that doesn't, which MathML sets in text style whatever the formula's style; an accent, or words over an arrow, is drawn the same in both styles. It puts such a formula in a span of class `displaystyle`, which `html-source.lua` makes `\displaystyle` at the start of the formula's TeX, the span gone. LaTeX sets it so, and the PDF filter's counts read it already (`formula_width`'s display flag). On a page, in an EPUB, and in a deck's reveal.js presentation, `target-blocks.lua` writes the MathML of a formula whose TeX opens with `\displaystyle`, such a step's or one from any source, in an `mstyle` that says so, which texmath doesn't write, laid out as texmath lays out a display, a sum's limits under and over it, but in the line, its TeX, `\displaystyle` and all, still the annotation the HTML reader reads back; not in a heading or a picture's description, whose text the writer reads (a picture's alt text, an EPUB's contents and a chapter's title), where the formula keeps its TeX, in text style. A Word file sets it as before: Pandoc's writer writes the same Office math with `\displaystyle` as without (measured). OpenStax sets the integral sign alone in display style in 54 of the 97 parts of *Calculus Volume 2*'s steps so set, the integrand in text style; once Pandoc has read the formula, which part was can't be told, so the whole formula is in display style, as the measurement Rob decided on had it (`\displaystyle` before each formula): in 20 of *Calculus Volume 2*'s 85 cells, a fraction OpenStax set in text style is drawn in display style beside its integral sign. No step sets a part in text style inside one in display style. Of the 851 tables of steps in OpenStax's public repositories, 35 hold such formulas, 130 of them: *Calculus Volume 2*'s 91 (85 cells and 6 formulas in reasons, in 26 of its 29 tables), *Calculus Volume 3*'s 37 (in 8 of its 17), and 2 in one table of the first volume of *Fizyka dla szkół wyższych*, whose `mtable` sets its cells in display style; 20 more are set so where it changes nothing (a relation, a number) and aren't marked. *Calculus Volume 2*'s chapter 3, unpacked, converted, and built on `main` and with these commits: 121 pages where 120, as Round 17 measured; 22 tables and formulas fitted, 2 by their spaces and 20 in smaller type, where 20, 1 and 19, the least at 75 percent either way; built in 596 and 592 seconds, side by side. Checked in the CNX suite (a step whose integral sign alone is in display style, in a cell and in a reason, marked, as are a limit's limits, a large operator by its `largeop`, and a fraction in a table in a cell that sets its cells so; a relation in display style, a fraction in a script under it, an accent, and words over an arrow not; the cells of a table set in display style, its fraction marked and its number not, nor a fraction in a reason's lines; and the converted page's MathML), and in the convert suite (the span made `\displaystyle` in a `markdown` target, and gone; the page's MathML and an EPUB's in an `mstyle`; the page read back as written; a Markdown source's sum laid out as a display, but a picture's description and a heading keeping their TeX; and, in Chromium, the formula marked drawn taller than the same one not), and in the decks suite (a formula opening with `\displaystyle` in display style in a deck's presentation and on its page), each failing with the code it covers taken out (Round 18 in PROJECT-NOTES).

64. **The dev-math books' steps have no rules either.** Rob, of item 58's offer (2026-10-24): "Go ahead and implement the fix for display style and the dev-math books, too." A table the unpacker lays out for where it stands (`layout_reason`'s `place`) in a dev-math book or *College Algebra with Corequisite Support 2e*, outside a Key Equations section (`steps_table`, `STEPS_STYLES`), gets the class `steps` beside OpenStax's own: most of them a worked example's steps (821 of *Prealgebra 2e*'s 860, in solutions), the rest a box of properties, a comparison in Key Concepts, a grid of questions and answers, or a problem laid out where it stands; *Physics*' grids of key terms, laid out for where they stand too, don't. Every target draws the class as it draws item 58's: `pdf-target.lua` sets `\toprule` and `\bottomrule` to nothing in the table's group (`UNRULED`), the table otherwise set as another layout table is, its formulas breaking as another table's do, and `page.css` takes the rules off its body, on a page and in an EPUB; a Word file had none. A PDF draws steps of either kind so only while they're a layout table (`is_layout`), as a page did already: steps that `table-headers.csv` makes a table of data, their role gone and their class kept, had kept only the rule under their head in a PDF, where a page drew all three. *Prealgebra 2e* has 860 such tables, *Elementary Algebra 2e* 943, *Intermediate Algebra 2e* 830, and the corequisite book 89 (unpacked with these commits, offline); *Precalculus 2e* and *Physics* none. *Prealgebra 2e*'s chapter 3, whose 110 layout tables are all steps: unpacked, converted, and built on `main` and with these commits, 141 pages where 143, each table of steps without its two rules and the space beside them; built in 632 and 631 seconds, side by side. OpenStax's own PDFs draw these tables with a rule under each row: its design for the dev-math books, as for *Precalculus 2e*, the corequisite book, the calculus books, and *Algebra 1*, gives each cell of a table classed `unstyled` a gray bottom border (`.os-table.os-unstyled-container > table > tbody > tr > td`, 0.09rem, #757474, in ce-styles' `styles/output/dev-math-pdf.css` at a592354), so there every step is ruled off from the next, where item 58's steps, a formula, have no rule; these are drawn with none, as offered, and a rule under each row is offered (open questions). Checked in the CNX suite (a dev-math book's steps, classed or not, of the class `steps`, its tables of data and those kept data tables not, nor another book's; the corequisite book's steps, and not its Key Equations, *Physics*' grid or Key Equations, or a byline; and the class on the converted page), in the PDF suite's fit book (no rule within 20 points of such steps' two lines, where a layout table that isn't steps has its two, and steps of either kind made a table of data again their three), and in the convert suite in Chromium (such steps' body's borders 0 pixels wide, another layout table's 1), each failing with the code it covers taken out.

65. **The PDF suite leaves no book's folder behind.** Found by the slides session (`SLIDES-PLAN.md`, Round 22, Found not changed) and given to this one by Rob (2026-10-24): since f3ac5cc, the PDF suite builds a book of pages in other languages in a folder beside its other books' (`build_languages`), which neither its clean-up nor its `--keep` message named, so each run left `pdf-tests-…-languages` in the temporary directory. Both read one list now (`BOOK_FOLDERS`), and a check sees that every folder whose name starts with the main book's is on it, failing with the languages book's left off the list.

66. **What a review of items 63 to 65's code found, and the fixes.** A separate agent read the round's code and tried it on small cases of its own (conversions, a page of *Calculus Volume 2* built to a PDF, the CNX suite), running no other suite, and found three defects, each real and each fixed with a check that fails under the mutation undoing it (Round 18 in PROJECT-NOTES). A formula opening with `\displaystyle` in a picture's description lost its alt text on a page and in an EPUB, and one in a heading its label in an EPUB's contents and its chapter's title: the writer reads their text, and a raw formula has none. `target-blocks.lua` now leaves a heading's and a picture's description's formulas as they are (`display_styles`, a walk from the top that doesn't go into them), in text style, their TeX the text. Steps of the class `steps` that `table-headers.csv` makes a table of data, as the unpacker's own report suggests for one with a header row, lost their top and bottom rules in a PDF, keeping only the one under their head, where the page drew all three; so did item 58's steps, class `formula-steps`, made so (item 64 now). And the convert suite's check of a marked formula drawn larger in Chromium turned a failure into a skip, a formula missing from the page timing out where the case asked whether Chromium starts; only Chromium failing to start skips the case now, and with it its other checks, Round 17's of the steps among them. It found four ways `_displayed` departed from MathML, none of them anywhere in OpenStax's sources, its stricter reading and the unpacker's agreeing on every cell of every step: a table of lines in a reason, which sets its cells in text style, given its table of steps' display style; a table in a cell with `displaystyle="true"` taken as text style; an accent, or words over an arrow, taken as changed by display style; and a large operator by its `largeop`, or one of the operator dictionary's large operators the set left out (∱, ∲, ∳, ⨋ to ⨜, ⨃, ⨅, and ⨉), not seen. Each is fixed as MathML has it, and the unpacker marks the same 130 formulas (a census, `r18/scripts/dispcensus.py`). And the docs: the class `steps` goes to every table the dev-math books lay out where they stand outside a Key Equations section, a worked example's steps for the most part, 821 of *Prealgebra 2e*'s 860, in solutions, and otherwise a box of properties, a comparison in Key Concepts, a grid of questions and answers, or a problem laid out so, which the docs now say; `page.css`'s comment left out the corequisite book; and a span of class `displaystyle` goes only where it has no attribute either. Left: when texmath can't read a formula opening with `\displaystyle`, Pandoc warns of it twice, `target-blocks.lua`'s writing it once and the writer's once, the formula then written as before. What it found sound: `_displayed`'s reading of nested `mstyle`s, a cell's `"false"` in a table's `"true"`, a script's base and its scripts, and `mroot`'s index; no formula wrapped twice; `#span.attributes`; the pattern that splits the written MathML, `%` in TeX, `gsub`'s two results, `prefer_ascii`, and a formula Pandoc can't convert; epubcheck and the Nu checker on its pages; a PDF of 37 marked formulas; a Word file's equations byte for byte the same; and the round's counts.

67. **Two other sessions' patches, tested on the texts they hadn't got, and merged.** Rob (2026-10-25): "each of the other sessions has a patch here for you to evaluate and, if appropriate, merge into your next mbox. Make any corrections or additions you deem appropriate. (You have access to texts that they do not, so they can't test as completely.) Another reason we are having you merge these is that the sidecar changes are breaking changes." The slides session's `math-report.mbox`, with `math-report-notes.md` for whoever tested it: `math.from_text` leaving a run cut short of its formula as text (OpenStax's ANOVA deck's `F = MS(Factor)/MS(Error)` had become the equation `F = MS` and text), and a deck's `math-repaired.csv` in Pandoc's TeX with its pages' repairs. The Instrumental Analysis session's `sidecar-bom.mbox`, with `sidecar-bom-handoff.md` for the session taking the sidecars over: a byte-order mark on every CSV written for a person, and every sidecar read with one at any line's start. Each commit is as its session made it, rebased onto d7306eb, the changelog's entries both kept and the testing page's lines merged a word at a time (`r18/rebase/merge3.py`). Tested here. **The cut-short rule** on 15 books, the filter before and after it run on the same Pandoc output: 7 from the Word corpus (1,765 files) and 8 unpacked from CNXML (*Introductory Statistics 2e*, *Introductory Business Statistics 2e*, *Statistics*, *Chemistry 2e*, *Principles of Economics 3e*, *Principles of Finance*, *Principles of Financial Accounting*, and *University Physics Volume 1*). Of 11,606 rows of `math-repaired.csv`, 77 are gone, each the start of a formula the run couldn't take whole (`X ~ G` of `X ~ G(.02)`, `P(A) = P` of `P(A) = P(B)`, `F = MS` of `F = MS(Factor)`), and 14 are new, a symbol alone (`ŷ`, `μ`, `Q₃`, `β`) in a run that's text now. One edge, a picture straight after a run, is item 68; a footnote straight after one doesn't count, its text not being in the line. **The deck report** on 13 decks built here from *Introductory Business Statistics 2e*'s Word chapters (`r19/decks/mkdecks.py`, 636 equations), with an `html` and a `source` target: 169 rows, all TeX, each under its deck's name and none twice, where `main`'s were the copies' 75 equations written out in one line; rows copied from it into `math-keep.csv` kept their equations in the copy and on the page, and their expressions text; an old keep sidecar, in one line, still kept its equations in the copy, unwarned; a copies-only run gave TeX, and one without Pandoc one line, without an error; decks named with what `safe_stem` replaces (`Ch 2: Descriptive Statistics`, `Ch 3 (Probability Topics)`) kept their rows; and two decks whose pages would be one (`Two Words` and `Two-Words`) stop the conversion, as they did. Two defects, both item 68's: that stop left no report at all, and a report written without Pandoc said its rows were TeX. **The sidecars** on *Introductory Business Statistics 2e* unpacked from CNXML and converted to pages, an EPUB, Word files, and a source copy, its `image-alt.csv` (twelve rows drafted for it) and `table-captions.csv` as Excel leaves them, a mark first, CRLF line ends, a report appended after a mark of its own, and rows appended from a file saved without a header after another (`r19/scripts/excelize.py`), against `main` with them plain (`r19/scripts/sccompare.py`): the pages, the EPUB, and the Word files the same but for the seven pages whose math the cut-short rule changed, every report the same rows with a mark first, and the drafted alt texts on the pages. The copy took none of them, in either build: item 71.

68. **What the math report needed.** A slides run whose conversion stops after the copies are written, as it does for two decks whose pages would be one, left no `math-repaired.csv`: with a conversion to follow, `deckrun` hands the copies' rows to `convert.py`'s report, which the stop never reached. `deckrun` writes them too now, unsaid, a report the conversion's replaces when it comes. A deck's report written without Pandoc, or holding an equation Pandoc couldn't read, said its rows were Pandoc's TeX; its hint says which are written out in one line, much as PowerPoint's linear format writes an equation, and only when some are (`deckrun.IN_ONE_LINE`, and `in_one_line` handed over for `convert.py`'s). And the cut-short rule took a picture straight after a run for a letter, by its description, which is the picture's text to the filter, and left the run text; a picture isn't in the line, so it doesn't count now (`expression_at`). The docs say what the patch left unsaid: a row written out in one line keeps its equation in the copy alone; a symbol in a run cut short is still a symbol on its own (`sₚ = 3cm`); and a display paragraph holding more than one equation, which the copy repairs one by one and the page reads as one formula, would take each of its rows to keep (none in a deck here).

69. **A header row repeated in a sidecar, and a sidecar setting naming a folder.** What the handoff left: appending a report to a sidecar, its header row and all, leaves a header row partway, which the filter and the patch's readers skip, and the readers that use `csv.DictReader` didn't. An HTML copy's of `image-alt.csv` and `bare-links.csv`, a Word copy's of `bare-links.csv`, and `split-pages.py`'s of `page-names.csv` each read it as a row, keyed on its column's name; they read through `sidecars.dict_rows` now, which skips a row whose first value is its first column's name. A Word copy's reader of `image-alt.csv` never took one, its keys being `stem/media/rIdN` (the one mutation of the round that changes nothing). And a sidecar setting naming a folder, which the patch's check of a sidecar's encoding opened as a file, stopping the run with a traceback, stops it saying so (`check_sidecar`); `sidecars.not_utf8` has nothing to say of a path that isn't a file, so a tool checking one says what it said before.

70. **A picture's alt text in a PDF is its characters, not TeX.** What the handoff left, the apostrophe the Instrumental Analysis session found in 27 of its book's 500 figures' `/Alt` ("E0\textquotesingle s"), measured on OpenStax's books and fixed with the rest of the writer's escapes (PANDOC-NOTES, the LaTeX writer's Images). Of OpenStax's 43,153 alt texts, 3,783 have a straight quote and 3,110 a curly double quote, and 96 of the 224 in *Prealgebra 2e*'s third chapter, built to a PDF, read "\textquotesingle" (258 times) or "\textbar" (6). `latexbuild.PDF_MACROS` declares the six commands Pandoc's writer writes for characters (`\textquotesingle`, `\textbar`, `\textless`, `\textgreater`, `\textasciitilde`, and `\textbackslash`) plain as those characters, wherever LaTeX makes text plain, and makes the alt key's `\^{}` a caret and its ligatures, curly quotes and dashes, the characters before the tagging reads it. The chapter's 224 alt texts have none left, 223 the page's alt text to the character, and its 141 pages and their text are the same; the one not to the character has a backquote, which the writer writes as itself and LaTeX sets as a left quote, so its `/Alt` has a left quote too (17 of OpenStax's alt texts have a backquote). The PDF suite's figure for it is a third with LaTeX's label in its caption, which its check of captions' shapes counts.

71. **A copy finds a picture's alt text in `media/` as the pages do.** Found testing the sidecars (item 67). The filter keys a picture whose path is in `media/` under its page's name (`qualify_media`), a rule for a Word file's media, every file's being `media/rIdN`, which holds as well for a page's picture in a book's own `media/` folder, as the CNX unpacker writes one; and `image-alt-missing.csv` gives that key, the path as Pandoc's reader writes it, a space as `%20` (PANDOC-NOTES, the HTML reader). The pages took such a row, and an HTML or a Markdown copy looked for the path alone, so a source target's copy of an unpacked OpenStax book kept none of the alt text decided for its pictures: of the statistics book's twelve rows, the pages took eleven and the copy none, in `main`'s build as in the series'. The twelfth was keyed by its file's name with a space, which the pages don't take either. A copy looks for the page's key first now (`sidecars.page_media_key`, the path encoded as Pandoc's readers encode it, `sidecars.pandoc_uri`), and for the path alone after. Rebuilt, the copy takes the eleven, and nothing else the run writes changes but the output check's notes of one alt text on different pictures, which take a page's picture and its copy's for two. Found, not changed: so do 348 of the book's 462 such notes in `main`'s build, the copy being written without its pictures, so that the check can't tell one is the other's file (`outputcheck.check_shared_alt`).

72. **What a review of the merge found.** A separate agent read the six commits as they then were, ran the unit, slides, split, and filter suites under Python 3.13 and the unit and slides suites under 3.9.25 (every changed file compiles; the checks needing pypdf or pikepdf skipped), and tried small cases of its own, and found no serious defect. Fixed, each with a check that fails under the mutation undoing it: a sidecar setting naming a folder (item 69); no check that a deck report's note of rows in one line stays away when every row is TeX, which the slides suite checks now, nor of the note in a run that converts decks, which a unit check calls `deck_math_report` for; the changelog's word that every escape is its character wherever LaTeX makes text plain, which holds for the six commands, the rest the alt key's alone (a heading's `x\^2` is "x̂2" in the PDF, as it was); the formats page's "as written", a no-break space being a space and a soft hyphen or a zero-width space nothing; the sidecars page's reading as though a row in one line kept an equation on the page; and the changelog's word that a Word copy's reader of `image-alt.csv` took a header row. Left, and written down (`SLIDES-PLAN.md`): a display paragraph holding more than one equation, a row each in the copy and one on the page (`docs/slides.md`, item 68); and two it thought plausible and didn't reproduce, a copies-only run reading equations with whatever Pandoc is first on the path, unchecked, and `math-keep.csv`'s warnings unsaid when the conversion stops after the copies are written. What it found sound: the cut-short rule's parenthesis and letter, a footnote after a run, the Lua `parse_csv`'s marks, a deck named `Ünit 1 (a).pptx` round-tripping its rows, every CSV writer and reader in `bin/`, `lib/`, and `util/` (but `table-census.py`'s standard output and the filters' own files, on purpose), and `PDF_MACROS` on a book of twelve pictures and a deck.

73. **A sidecar Excel saved as plain "CSV" is rewritten as UTF-8.** Rob (2026-10-25): "if I understand correctly, this is a breaking change and old sidecars will no longer work until they are updated to use UTF-8. Is that right? Can we detect and automatically rewrite sidecars that are in the old format so there is an easy upgrade path for users with old sidecars?" For a sidecar it isn't, measured on a one-page book with `image-alt.csv` and `table-captions.csv`: one in UTF-8, with the mark or without it, reads the same on d7306eb, before item 67's patch, as on f7969c2, after it; and one Excel saved as plain "CSV", in Windows-1252, never worked. On d7306eb each character outside ASCII was a U+FFFD in the page's alt text and caption ("Caf&#xFFFD; &#xFFFD; a r&#xFFFD;sum&#xFFFD;&#xFFFD;s"), and then the run stopped with a traceback, in `unreviewed_rows`; on f7969c2 it stops before converting, naming the line (item 69's check). What changed for a person's files is the reports' mark, which a script reading a report as `utf-8` finds before the first column's name, as the changelog's Breaking entry says, and it says now that sidecars need no change. So such a sidecar is rewritten now, by the run before anything is converted and by `table-headers.py`, `split-pages.py`, `util/remediate.py`, `util/round-trip-decks.py`, and `util/shortdoi.py` before they read it, `shortdoi.py --dry-run` reading it so and writing nothing (`sidecars.read_code_page` and `rewrite_as_utf8`, into which `sidecars.not_utf8` is folded). Its lines that aren't UTF-8 are read in Windows-1252 or in Mac OS Roman, the code pages Windows Excel's and Excel for Mac's plain "CSV" use in the US and Western Europe, as others report them (Donat Studios' "CSV: An Encoding Nightmare", 2016 to 2022; not tried here, where there's no Excel), whichever reads them with fewer characters misplaced (`sidecars.misplaced`): a sign or a letter where a misread one sits and text's seldom does, such as a Latin capital after a small letter ("donÕt" is "don’t" read in the wrong one) or between capitals before a small one ("OÕNeill"), a small letter before a capital ("ìHello"), a Latin letter between digits ("1990ñ2000"), a letter that can only be a misread sign standing alone ("a Ð b" for an en dash), ä after a number ("5 ä" for "5 ‰"), a third letter outside ASCII in a row (Cyrillic read as Latin), a mark between letters ("M‰rz"), a spacing accent ("¯ 25 mm" for "Ø 25 mm"), a superscript after a space, or ¹ between a digit and a letter ("p ² 0.05" for "p ≤ 0.05", "2¹r" for "2πr"), and ¡ with no ! after it ("100 ¡C" for "100 °C"), the five characters both read alike counting for nothing (M±SD). Where they have as many, the line ends decide, a CR alone Mac OS Roman's, as Excel 2011 for Mac saved them, and CR LF Windows-1252's, Excel 2016 for Mac ending lines as Windows does, as a Microsoft Q&A thread of 2016-11-30 reports. A line that is UTF-8, as a report's rows appended to the sidecar are, is kept as it is, and shown in the warning when it holds a character outside ASCII, since a line in the code page can happen to be UTF-8 ("Gauß’s" in Windows-1252 is UTF-8's "Gauߒs", "20 Å²" its "20 Ų", and "jusqu’à" in Mac OS Roman its "jusquՈ"); a file that's UTF-8 throughout isn't rewritten at all. The file as it was is kept beside it (`image-alt.csv.windows-1252` or `.mac-roman`, then `.2` and on), never written over; the new one is written beside it and moved into its place (`sidecars._replace`), its owner and mode kept, so a write that fails leaves it as it was, with no copy beside it; and the run warns, showing ten of the lines read in the code page and ten of those kept as UTF-8, a character that shows as nothing by its number (`<U+00A0>`). A file whose lines, two of them or more, all read with a character misplaced in each code page stops the run instead, saying how each reads them, and so do one with a byte Windows-1252 has no character for whose lines all read with one misplaced in Mac OS Roman, a control character, UTF-16, as Excel's "Unicode Text" saves a file, a read-only file, and on Windows one Excel has open. Measured on 58 lines written here to read like alt text and captions, in English with curly quotes, dashes, and signs, and in French, Spanish, German, Italian, Portuguese, Dutch, Danish, and Swedish, each saved alone in each code page: Windows-1252's 58 read right, and Mac OS Roman's 55 it can hold with a CR alone; with CR LF, 48 of the 55, the other 7 read as Windows-1252, their characters as plausible there ("señor" as "se–or", "schön" as "schšn", "Chloë’s" as "Chlo‘Õs", "1º" as "1¼", "« Bonjour »" as "Ç Bonjour È"). All in one file read right in each code page with each line end, and none stopped. On the reviewers' 553 lines (item 74), each alone: Windows-1252's 445 of 457 read right, 5 are UTF-8 by chance, read so and not rewritten ("JOSÉ’S FARM" is UTF-8's "JOSɒS FARM"), and 7 read as Mac OS Roman, a letter's name ("la letra Ñ"), a superscript after a space ("¹H NMR"), Portuguese's "Exmª", and Norwegian's "HiØs" among them; Mac OS Roman's 454 of 461 with a CR alone, those but the superscript missed, "ÅÄÖ åäö" stopping, and "L’école" UTF-8 by chance, and 319 of 461 with CR LF. The third reviewer's 18 files of several lines each, in English, German, French, Catalan, and Swedish, all read right. Of files of three lines in other code pages, Russian in Windows-1251, Greek in Windows-1253, and Japanese in Shift JIS stop, and so does "CSV (MS-DOS)"'s "Müller" in a file of one; Czech and Polish in Windows-1250, Turkish in Windows-1254 ("Ýstanbul"), and "CSV (MS-DOS)" with a line that reads clean in Mac OS Roman ("Stra·e") are rewritten wrong, the warning showing each. A one-page book whose two sidecars Excel for Mac saved, Mac OS Roman with CR LF, converts with "Café – a résumé’s red square" as its alt text and "Prices – cafés’ year by year" as its caption. The docs: `docs/sidecars.md`'s "A sidecar that isn't UTF-8", with what it can get wrong and the steps for saving one as UTF-8 by hand, in Excel, in LibreOffice (its character sets' names read from its library here), and with `iconv`; `docs/troubleshooting.md`; and the changelog's Breaking and Fixed entries.

74. **What the reviews and the mutations found.** Three separate agents reviewed the rewrite, each trying text of its own in both code pages and both line ends: the first two whole versions as they then were, the third the last revision of `misplaced` and its checks. Fixed, each with a check: ordinary text read in the wrong code page or stopping the run ("1.54 Å", "M±SD", "Ø 25 mm", "p ≤ 0.05", "100 °C", "yen (¥)", "World Bank.¹", "25˚C", "2πR"), which is why the two readings are compared by how many characters each misplaces, the five characters both read alike count for nothing, and spacing accents, superscripts, and ¥ have rules of their own; a report's UTF-8 rows read again in the code page ("H₂O", "X̄"), kept as they are now and shown; a write that failed partway leaving the sidecar cut short, now written beside it and moved into place; `shortdoi.py --dry-run` writing; copies piling up beside a file that couldn't be written, now removed when the write fails; and a rewrite as root giving a person's file to root, its owner and mode kept now. The third found that three of the last revision's four new rules made things worse on its 248 lines of alt text in English, Spanish, French, German, Portuguese, Catalan, Italian, and the Nordic languages: a superscript between a digit and a letter ("10³m", "6.02×10²³mol", ² being a digit to Python) and any small letter after a digit ("2ƒ(x)") turned whole English Windows-1252 files into Mac OS Roman, Mac OS Roman's reading of them seldom counting anything; ä alone took German's "o. ä." and "u. ä." for Mac OS Roman's ‰, misreading a file or stopping it, as two lists of Swedish letters stopped; and the small letter after a digit stopped Mac OS Roman files with French or Catalan ordinals ("Le 2è étage", "5è curs"), whose è is a byte Windows-1252 has no character for. Narrowed as it suggested: only à and û after a digit, Mac OS Roman's ‡ and ˚ read as Windows-1252; ä only after a number, its ‰; and of the superscripts only ¹ between a digit and a letter, its π. On its lines, each alone in three setups, that reads 590 of 656 right, where the commit before the revision read 586 and the revision 566, and its 18 whole files all right, where the revision read 6; on the earlier lines it reads none worse than the revision but "The ‰ rate" and "The ‰ symbol" with CR LF, which the commit before read wrong too. Kept: a capital between capitals before a small letter, which reads Mac OS Roman's "O’Neill" and "D’Angelo" with CR LF right and "Die TÜVs" wrong. It found too that three checks would pass with the behavior broken, now each with a check of its own: the small letter after a digit ("Table 1à", "A 45û angle"), a run of letters of any script ("ªâð", Ukrainian's "Євр" in Windows-1251 read as Windows-1252, which counts now and didn't when only Latin letters made a run), and the one with fewer misplaced winning whatever the line ends prefer (a Mac OS Roman file with CR LF). Then 97 mutations of the rewrite and of the tools' wiring, each a rule or a step taken out or loosened, run on the checks that should catch it: all caught, one, the per mille rule's guard at a line's start, once its case ("ä 52") had a check of its own. Four that an earlier set missed found two checks wanting, now there (one line misplaced in both among two not stopping the run, and the copy kept beside the sidecar failing partway), and entries that did nothing, now gone: superscripts, the soft hyphen, ´, and ` among the marks text has between letters, each meeting another rule first, and ª and º among the small letters it has before a capital, which aren't Latin letters. Left, and written down in `docs/sidecars.md`: Mac OS Roman with CR LF whose characters read as plausibly in Windows-1252, a letter's name alone, a line in the code page that happens to be UTF-8, and code pages other than the two.

75. **A sidecar in UTF-8 without the byte-order mark is given one.** Rob (2026-10-26), of what item 73's round offered: "Yes, also add the BOM to old UTF-8 sidecars that are missing it." Excel opens a CSV as UTF-8 only when it starts with the mark; without one it reads the file in the computer's code page, "é" as "Ã©", and a sidecar saved from Excel again, even as "CSV UTF-8", keeps that as its text. Every CSV a run writes for a person has had the mark since item 67's merge (c593535), but a sidecar made before, or saved by a text editor, may not. Now the run, and the tools that read a sidecar on their own, give one that's UTF-8 without the mark, and holds a character outside ASCII, the mark (`sidecars._mark`, through `rewrite_as_utf8`, once `read_code_page` finds it UTF-8), and say so: "image-alt.csv had no byte-order mark, which Excel needs to open it as UTF-8: one is added, and nothing else in it changes," its lines outside ASCII shown as they read, ten of them, since a file Excel saved in a code page can happen to read as UTF-8 ("Gauß’s" in Windows-1252 as "Gauߒs", item 73), which with the mark Excel shows so too. It's written beside it and moved into its place (`_replace`), its owner and mode kept, its bytes otherwise as they were, every line end and a mark partway among them. One of ASCII alone is left as it is: Excel reads it the same either way, and the first version, which marked every sidecar, broke a script that reads one as `utf-8`, the headers suite's own check of an unmatched row, which read `table-headers.csv` after a run and found `\ufeffkey` where `key` was; the changelog's Breaking entry says such a script finds the mark in a sidecar that holds a character outside ASCII and should read it as `utf-8-sig`. Left as they are too, nothing said, each reading the same either way: an empty file, a read-only one (no write bit, for root as for anyone, `sidecars._writable`), another user's, whose owner a run that isn't root can't keep, one with another link to it, one Windows holds open, and one no longer UTF-8 by the time it's marked. `shortdoi.py --dry-run` reads with `read_code_page` and writes nothing, as before. Checked by the unit suite's code-page checks and by the convert suite's Excel case, a sidecar a text editor saved, LF line ends and no mark, given it end to end, its alt text on the page and nothing left beside it; and 110 mutations of the code-page rewrite, the mark, and the tools' wiring, each run on the checks that should catch it, all caught. The docs: `docs/sidecars.md`'s "In a spreadsheet" and its opening, the changelog's Breaking entry, and `docs/architecture.md`'s line for `lib/sidecars.py`, which named neither rewrite.

76. **What a review of it found.** A separate agent read the commit as it then was and ran the unit, headers, split, convert, slides, filter, CNX, unpack, audit, EPUB, LaTeX, and PDF suites with `_mark` noting each file it marked: marks happened only in the new checks, another convert case, and three slides cases' `math-keep.csv`, none of them reading the file back as plain `utf-8`. It tried bytes of its own (every line end, a mark partway, NUL, U+2028, a quoted field across lines, links, a FIFO, `/dev/null`, a file of 100 MB), each coming out as the mark and the bytes it had, and found every Python reader of a sidecar using `utf-8-sig` and both Lua filters that read one taking marks off. Fixed, each with a check: a file Excel saved in Windows-1252 that happens to read as UTF-8 was marked with nothing shown, so the note shows its lines outside ASCII; a run that isn't root gave another user's file its own user and group, as Round 20's rewrite already did, so such a file isn't marked, nor one with another link to it, which the replace would part, and `_owned` keeps a rewritten file's group where it can't keep its owner; a run as root, which `os.access` lets write anything, marked a file with no write bit, and rewrote one from a code page, so `_writable` asks the mode too; a file that stopped being UTF-8 before it was marked ended the run with a traceback; the checks' files ended their lines with LF alone, with no mark partway, so that reading with universal newlines, or adding a last line end, passed them; the convert check didn't count a temporary file left beside the sidecar; and `docs/sidecars.md`, `docs/conversion-settings.md` (from `bin/schema-conversion.yaml`), and `table-headers.py` still said sidecars are never written. The changelog's Breaking entry had read as though the mark were for scripts.

77. **Every sidecar made UTF-8 with the mark, and one the run can't change read as it is.** Rob (2026-10-26), having pushed `sidecar-mark.mbox` (112249f and d5f6748): "Go ahead and make the necessary changes to upgrade all sidecars to UTF-8 and read UTF-8 for all of them. Let's break everything now, not later. We're still in beta and there aren't a lot of users yet (only me, AFAIK). If there are files owned by other users but in the correct directory and readable by us, then we should use them (with an appropriate warning). Likewise, if a sidecar exists but isn't readable by us, then we should warn about that, too. Basically, assume that users will be sloppy about permissions but files in the directory are there for a reason." So a sidecar of ASCII alone, or an empty one, is given the mark too (`sidecars._mark`), the run saying so in a line ("…had no byte-order mark: one is added, as Excel's "CSV UTF-8" writes one, and nothing else in it changes."), and a script that reads sidecars as plain `utf-8` finds the mark in every one at once, not in one that comes to hold a character outside ASCII later; the headers suite, which read `table-headers.csv` so, reads it as `utf-8-sig`. One the run can't rewrite (`sidecars._kept`: another user's, unless the run is root's, which keeps its owner, that alone said of it, and its folder where that can't be written; one on a disk mounted read-only, that alone; read-only, which is no write bit, for root too; one with a write bit that this user may not write, or, for root, can't (marked immutable, say); one with another link to it; one in a folder this user can't write, and for one in a code page, whose file as it was is kept beside a link, the link's folder too) or fails to (one held open, a disk full) is read all the same and left as it is, the run saying why and what would let a run rewrite it (replace another user's with a copy of your own made with `cp -p`, which keeps its mode, or ask its owner to save it as UTF-8 with the mark; copy the book to a disk that can be written; make it writable; give the user running this permission to write it; make it a file of its own; make its folder writable; close the program that has it open, by the error, Windows' "access denied" given both that and permission), as a note for one of ASCII alone: one in a code page, which had stopped the run, is read in it, and one in UTF-8 without the mark, which had been passed over without a word, as it is. One that's there and can't be read (`sidecars._look`: one this user may not read, which had stopped the run with a `PermissionError` traceback, its owner named when it's someone else; a link to a file that isn't there, at a sidecar's own name, which had been passed over without a word; a folder, which `convert.py` stops at, saying so, as before, and the tools go on without; a pipe or a device, which had kept the run waiting for good) is gone without, the run saying so, and never written over; one that can't be read for another reason, a disk's error or a network share gone stale, stops the run, saying so, where it had been a traceback, since going on would make a book without what it holds. A setting naming a link to nothing stops the run, as one naming nothing does, saying where the link leads. Every reader in Python reads a sidecar through `sidecars.opened`, its text as `sidecars.text` reads it, not with `open`, so that one left in a code page is read in it and one that can't be read is read as none: `convert.py`'s count of drafted rows and its bare links, `table-headers.py`, `split-pages.py`, the Word, page, and deck copies' readers, `languages.read_rows`, `math_keep_rows`, and `shortdoi.py`. The filters, which read a sidecar's bytes, are given a copy in UTF-8 of one left in a code page (`sidecars.filter_copy`, `convert.FILTER_SIDECARS`, which a unit check holds to the sidecars the filters read), the filter's warnings naming `image-alt.csv` by `IMAGE_ALT_NAME`, not the copy. `table-headers.py` and `split-pages.py` don't check again what `convert.py` checked (`--sidecar-checked`, a setting `convert.py` alone passes), which said each warning twice. `shortdoi.py` writes the sidecar in its place, as it always had, so that it keeps its owner, its mode, and its other names, a note saying so of another user's; it keeps the file as it was beside it before asking anything, under the next free name (`.bak`, or `.bak.2` and on where an earlier run's is there, which it had written over); it stops before asking anything when the sidecar can't be read, can't be written (read-only, on a disk mounted read-only, or not to be opened to write), or can't be kept, and a dry run says that a run would stop; and a write that fails after the lookups, a disk full, puts the original back where it can and prints the shortDOIs found, to add by hand. A rewritten sidecar keeps its extended attributes on Linux, a person's own, its access control list, and its SELinux label, not those that describe its old contents (`_owned`, `KEPT_ATTRIBUTES`), and is flushed to the disk before it's moved into place; and `round-trip-decks.py` and `adopt-pages.py` skip a sidecar they can't read, where they had copied it with a traceback. Tried as an unprivileged user (`claude`, uid 999) on a small book whose sidecars root owns: `main` stopped at a read-only `image-alt.csv` in Windows-1252 ("can't be rewritten as UTF-8: it's read-only"), and with a `bare-links.csv` only root may read, stopped with the `PermissionError` traceback; with the change, the run said why for each, read `image-alt.csv` and root's `table-captions.csv` (UTF-8 without the mark, anyone may write it) as written, the page's alt text and caption with their dashes, went on without `bare-links.csv`, and gave the user's own `slide-titles.csv`, ASCII alone, the mark. Checked by the unit suite's code-page checks and a new group, every reader of every kind of sidecar reading one read-only in Windows-1252 as written and one it can't read as none; the convert suite's Excel case (read-only sidecars in Windows-1252, the filter's and the pre-pass's among them, read as written, said once, the filter's warning naming `image-alt.csv`; a pipe and a link to nothing gone without; one of ASCII alone given the mark) and a new case, a read-only `page-names.csv` in Windows-1252 naming a page and said once, though the split reads it too; the split suite's names case; `check_shortdoi`; and a group run as `nobody` under `setpriv` where root runs the suite, root's sidecars read by another user. Not tried: Windows, where Excel is reported to lock a file it has open, so that the rewrite fails and the file is read as it is. The docs: `docs/sidecars.md` (the opening, "In a spreadsheet", "One the run can't change, or can't read", and "A sidecar that isn't UTF-8"), `docs/troubleshooting.md`'s three new entries, `docs/bare-links.md`, the changelog's Breaking and Fixed entries, the settings reference, `docs/architecture.md`, and `docs/testing.md`.

78. **What two reviews of it found.** Separate agents read the commit, the second after the first's findings were fixed; each ran the sidecar checks as root and as uid 1000 and 999, and the run itself as an unprivileged user on books whose sidecars root owns. Fixed, each with a check but where said. Another user's file the user can't write either, root's 0644 file to anyone else, was said to be read-only, which it isn't, and the advice was to have its owner run the conversion, which, root's or anyone's, makes the book's outputs theirs, so that the next run by someone else fails writing them (a run as root first left `page.json` root's, and the user's run stopped at it); then "replace it with a copy of your own", by `cp` and `mv`, made a colleague's shared sidecar the user's, its mode 0644, so the colleague couldn't write it, and hid that its folder couldn't be written, which the copy needs. Now another user's file is said to be theirs, its folder too where that can't be written, and what to do is replace it with a copy made with `cp -p`, which keeps its mode, or ask its owner to save it as UTF-8 with the mark; read-only means no write bit; one with a write bit this user may not write is said to be that, one root can't write (marked immutable, say) that, and one on a disk mounted read-only that alone; and besides the patched checks, a real run as `nobody` under `setpriv`, where root runs the suite, its module copied to /tmp and probed first, so that a clone or a TMPDIR uid 65534 can't reach skips it, saying why. `shortdoi.py` wrote a file the run had just said it leaves as it is; with a `.bak` it couldn't write, it asked the service and then stopped with a traceback, the lookups lost; and refusing another user's file, as the first fix had it, broke a colleague's group-writable sidecar in a shared folder, which `main` wrote in place. Now it writes in its place, as it always had, keeping the sidecar's owner, mode, and other names, and says so of another user's; it keeps the file as it was before asking anything, under the next free name (`.bak.2` where an earlier run's `.bak` is there, which it had written over); it stops before asking anything when the sidecar can't be read, written (read-only, on a disk mounted read-only, or not to be opened to write, as on Windows one Excel has open, not tried here), or kept, and a dry run says a run would stop; and a write that fails after the lookups, a disk full or an append-only file, puts the original back where it can and prints the shortDOIs found, where it had been a traceback. A setting naming a link to nothing went on without it, where one naming nothing stops the run, as the docs say: it stops again, saying where the link leads; only the default name's is gone without. `util/round-trip-decks.py` and `bin/adopt-pages.py` said a sidecar couldn't be read and then copied it, with a traceback: they skip it (`adopt-pages.py` tried as an unprivileged user, neither checked by a suite), and `round-trip-decks.py` copies `project.yaml` as before, whose loss would otherwise be silent. What to do was chosen by the error's class, every `PermissionError` "close any program that has it open": it's chosen by the error now; on Windows a sharing or lock violation (32, 33) is a program's, but replacing a file another program has open is "access denied" (5), as Git for Windows and LLVM have found, so that gets both remedies (not tried here); EACCES and EPERM are permission. A file that can't be read for another reason than permission, a disk's error or a network share gone stale, made the run go on without it, a book without what it holds: it stops the run, as it did before. The rewrite, a new file, lost the file's extended attributes, an access control list among them on Linux, which marking every sidecar would do to every one: `_owned` copies a person's own (`user.*`), the access control list, and the SELinux label, not those that describe the old contents, as integrity measurement's do (a user attribute and an access control list giving uid 1000 read and write kept, a trusted attribute not); and the new file is flushed to the disk before it's moved into place, which a crash on XFS could otherwise leave empty (checked by counting the flushes). The changelog and docs said a pipe and a link to nothing had stopped the run with a traceback, where a pipe had kept it waiting for good and a link to nothing at a sidecar's own name was passed over without a word; stated Excel's lock on Windows as fact; and claimed `shortdoi.py` caught another user's file on Windows, where nothing tells whose a file is. And the tests had covered none of `--sidecar-checked`, `IMAGE_ALT_NAME`, a configured link to nothing, or the filters' copy of `math-keep.csv`, and the uid 1000 the checks give a person's file (since Round 20) took the group down as any other user, which uses the user's own uid now. Left as it was, a judgment call for Rob: another user's sidecar that already has the mark is read without a word, nothing about it needing a person; and the run never rewrites another user's sidecar, even one it may write, where `shortdoi.py`, asked to write one, does, in place. Then 85 mutations of the reads, the rewrite, the warnings, and the tools' wiring, each run on the checks that should catch it: all caught, on the code as delivered. Found by an earlier run of them, not the reviews: the unit check of a pipe gave up with a `TimeoutError`, which is an `OSError`, which the reader took for a file it couldn't read and opened the pipe again, waiting for good (it raises an error of its own now, and again every five seconds); a reader of a sidecar whose lines end with a CR alone, which Excel 2011 for Mac wrote and a rewrite keeps, had no check of its own; and the check of the write bits in main's `_writable`, which `_kept` had come to call, had become one `_kept` makes first, so `_writable` went, `_kept` asking `os.access` itself.

79. **Pandoc 3.13: what it broke, mended, and what reading it found for older Pandocs.** Rob (2026-10-26), having tested `sidecar-utf8.mbox`: "Tests passed (2 skipped) with Pandoc 3.12.1. However, on the newly-released Pandoc 3.13 some tests fail": the convert suite's lists in quotations through Word, the LaTeX suite's list with a formula, a table, and a rule read back from Word, and the decks suite's notes slides. 3.13 and its source were fetched and its changes read before anything was run (PANDOC-NOTES). The decks failure was the suite's own: 3.13's PowerPoint writer writes the notes slide's color map that the `pptx` target had added (25bef2d, #11960), and the target already left one alone; the check expects the target's count only before 3.13 now. The other two were one change in the Word writer and reader (#9197): an item's later paragraphs are unnumbered and indented like the item's text, where 3.12.1 numbered each in a blank-marker list, and the reader keeps a paragraph so indented in the item and ends every list it has open at anything else. A formula in an item, which the target still numbered as the older writer did, ended the item; so did a table, a rule, or a figure in it, the figure paired with its caption now that its picture isn't numbered, its caption's words a quotation inside the caption; and so did a quotation or code in an item, which the target indents beyond the item's text so its depth survives. What followed came back as quotations, and the list's later items as a list of their own. **The change**: `docxtarget` follows the writer it runs (`pandoc_version`, `CONTINUATION_PANDOC`), a formula in an item given the item's indent from 3.13, and its file says so in a document variable (`TextbookImproverContinuations`); reading such a file, `docxrepair.item_paragraphs` mirrors 3.13's reader (its open lists, and which of them the reader has ended at something the item holds), and `mark_continuations` lines a quote's or code's paragraph up with the item's text, marked with its depth, numbers as a list paragraph (an id with no definition, and no indent) each paragraph the reader would keep in no list, and takes the indent off a held figure's paragraphs and a held table's caption; `apply_list_tables` puts a figure back in its item as it does a table or a rule; with a reader before 3.13, every such paragraph is a list paragraph, as an older file's are. A paragraph in the writer's form that no open list lines up with (what follows a heading in an item; the words of an item the writer numbered nowhere, in a file written before) is a list paragraph at its level, as 3.12.1 had it, not a quotation. The first review (item 80) found more the reader ends a list at, each mirrored now: a note, whose paragraphs it reads at the reference (`notePartsToBlocks` keeps no list open across them), after which the item's paragraphs before the note are list paragraphs too, since the reader folds one only into the item's own paragraph just before it (`handleListParagraphs`); a caption after a table, which it pairs with the table (`addCaptioned`, mirrored as `docxtarget.captioned_parts`, which also counts the reader's figures for the target's marks); and a table's cells, read from cell to cell. A figure of two pictures, which the writer writes as a table, is held as one. And the target counts the blocks the reader gives back after something an item holds as the reader groups list items (`flatToBullets'`), a list the item holds split where its items hold a table too, and the reading puts an outer item's blocks back first (item 80). **With either Pandoc**: a table's caption in a list item, which the writer gives the item's Compact style (a `Plain` caption takes the list's style over Table Caption), read back as a paragraph beside the caption the reader took from `w:tblCaption`, the caption twice and the list ended there (Table 11.21, item 11-5's list split in two): the target styles it Table Caption now (`table_captions`), and reading a file written before makes the paragraph the table's caption; a quotation's mark, `tiqItemQuote1x1`, was left on a page as an empty anchor where it was the page's only change (`nest_item_quotes` didn't count it); an item that opens with anchors before a list, as the solution to Example 2.17 has its parts, or with a table, a figure of two pictures, or a rule, got no number from either writer, lost in Word and in reading (`_number_anchored`: the anchors a paragraph of their own, an empty paragraph before the table or rule, as the writer puts one before a list); a formula alone in a `Plain`, in a tight item, ended the list; and a list in an item came apart where both items held a table or a rule, or the outer item had a paragraph after the inner list (the count above). **Found reading 3.13's changes, fixed for older Pandocs**: a table of 10 columns or more with a line break or a block in a cell was the text `[TABLE]` in a `markdown` target (#11945; written as HTML now, as a table with merged cells is); and a PDF stopped on a definition list's term with an `=` in it, which LaTeX's tagging took for keys in `\item[...]` (#11954; braced now, as 3.13 does). Documented, not changed: 3.13 reads YAML 1.2, so `toc: no` gives a contents page and a key given twice stops the build (measured). **Measured** on *Introductory Business Statistics 2e*'s HTML, 169 pages, through the Word target and back, compared with `util/compare-output.py` against the book converted directly: with the old code, 68 pages came back the same with either Pandoc; 3.12.1 left 21 pages' lists unlike the source's (10 with a list too many or too few) and 10 pages' figures pictures, and 3.13 25 pages' lists (16), quotations on 10 pages, and figures out of their lists. With the new code, 72 pages come back the same, written and read with 3.13, with 3.12.1, or written with 3.13 and read with 3.12.1; no page has a quotation; and the lists of only 5 pages differ with 3.13, 6 with 3.12.1 reading. The 5 are key-terms pages, whose definitions hold lists, which come back after their definitions, splitting the definition list (3 lists come back as 5, say), with either Pandoc, the old code too; the sixth, *2-2*, has a paragraph between two lists in an item, which reading before 3.13 puts in the first list's last item, the second list then joining it, one list fewer. From a file written with 3.13, whichever reads it, every figure is back in its item but the 7 that open theirs (2.36, 3.22, 8.18 to 8.22), which come back as their pictures and captions; from one written with 3.12.1, figures on 10 pages come back as pictures, as before. The rest of each run's differences, the same before and after, are pictures under Word's names (73 pages), word counts (36), formulas (7), and one page's tables, as a book read back from Word has them. What's left, each a limit the docs state: an item's paragraph after a list in it that comes back a list paragraph goes into that list's last item (with a Pandoc reading before 3.13, and with 3.13 where the item holds a table, a rule, or a figure before that list, or a note in the inner list's item; TI's 3.12.1 path did this before); a caption of more than one paragraph keeps one in the caption; a figure or table in a div with more in it, a credit say, still ends the list with 3.13; a list in a note comes back as paragraphs, and a list in a definition after the definition, as Pandoc's reader reads them (both versions; PANDOC-NOTES); a definition list in an item comes back as its term and a paragraph; a figure that opens its item comes back its picture and caption, the writer numbering the picture's paragraph; a figure of two pictures in an item written before 3.13 still ends the list; and an item that opens with a quotation, or holds a table in a quotation, comes back wrong with either Pandoc, as before. Not tried in Word or with a screen reader.

80. **What two reviews and the mutations of it found.** A separate agent read the round's diff with both Pandocs and their source and round-tripped its own pages through the Word target under 3.13, 3.12.1, and each written with one and read with the other; a second read the code as fixed, the same way. The first found, each now fixed with a check that fails without the fix: a figure an item held, if it had no caption or held two pictures, had no Captioned Figure paragraph, and its mark was resolved to the next captioned figure anywhere, so `apply_list_tables` moved a figure outside every list into one, the list after it joined on (a mark is resolved only inside its own bookmark range now, a figure with no caption isn't held, the item keeping its picture as any paragraph, and one of two pictures is held as the table the writer makes of it); the quotation mark of item 79, on every page whose quote was its only change, with 3.12.1 too; a figure of two pictures in an item lost its caption to the item, which reading took for an item's paragraph and numbered, so the reader no longer paired it with the table before it (`captioned_parts` mirrors the reader's pairing now, both ways); a note in an item still split the list with 3.13, the reader keeping no list open across the note's paragraphs; code in a quotation in a quotation after a table in an item came back a quotation too deep, `quoted_code` not knowing the table was the item's; and a list in a table's cell, written with 3.13 and read with 3.12.1, split, reading having passed over every cell. In the docs, a table's caption is Compact in any list item, not a tight one's only, and with the caption move it still came back twice; "reads back with either" wasn't so for the mark, the note, or the cell; and the figure count the notes described wasn't the reader's for a figure with no caption or of two pictures. And nine mutations got past the unit checks, two of them changing real round trips (a rule an item holds ignored, and the notes part read without `every`): each has a check now. Then 58 mutations of the target's and the reading's code, the Markdown filter's, and the PDF filter's, each run on the checks that should catch it with the Pandoc it concerns: 55 caught; of the three that got past, a test of a quote's style before a held table was redundant once the caption needed no indent of its own, and went; a heading in an item, which 3.13 indents like the item's text, had a fixture without the indent, now with it; and the target's styling of a table's caption, which reading an older file makes up for, is checked in the Word file itself. Each was caught then. The second review, of the code so fixed, found, each fixed now with a check that fails without the fix: the target counted an item's figures before the run took out the bookmarks nothing links to, an `<img>`'s id among them, and a picture's paragraph holding one is no figure to the count (`drawing_alone`) but is one to the reader once it's gone, so each held figure after such a picture was taken for the one before it, and a figure outside every list moved into one, the next list's blocks with it (the bookmarks are pruned before the figures are counted now, the target's own marks kept, and the count made in the file as the reading will have it, the marks gone and the bookmarks moved into paragraphs);
